# Documentation Audit — 2026-06-17

Full-repository documentation audit (markdown, Rust/Python docstrings, inline comments, notebook prose) verified against the code/repo state.

## Method

Run as a 40-unit agent swarm (one focused auditor per doc/code boundary) feeding an adversarial verification stage: every candidate finding was independently re-checked against the code by a second agent instructed to *refute* it. Only `confirmed` findings appear below.

- **Units audited:** 40  |  **Raw findings:** 109  |  **Confirmed:** 106  |  **Refuted/uncertain:** 3

- **By severity (verifier-corrected):** high 8, medium 41, low 57 (critical 0)

- **By category:** factual-drift 63, incompleteness 15, internal-inconsistency 13, stale-content 10, broken-reference 5


## ⚠ Beyond documentation — code/repo issues this audit surfaced

These are not doc edits; they require a code/repo decision.

1. **`bench run -i all` silently mislabels HashTrie measurements.** With `-i all -a leapfrog-triejoin`, the cross-product loop reaches the `HashTrie` arm and calls `run_benchmark_hash` for *any* algorithm (`kermit/src/main.rs:1581`), which always runs `hash_join` (`:1188`) yet stamps the report's algorithm axis with the passed-in algorithm name (`:1117`). The in-code comment at `main.rs:1573-1575` asserting this cannot happen is false (the `supports_algorithm` gate at `:132` is permissive whenever either selector is `All`). `-i all -a all` additionally panics on incompatible pairs (`db.rs:308-317`). **Only single explicit pairs are safe:** `-i tree-trie -a leapfrog-triejoin`, `-i column-trie -a leapfrog-triejoin`, `-i hash-trie -a hash-triejoin`.

2. **The WatDiv binary is committed, not gitignored.** `kermit-rdf/vendor/watdiv/bin/Release/watdiv` (360,112 bytes) is git-tracked at HEAD (added in `ca0815c`); the `**/watdiv` `.gitignore` rule is inert because the file was force-added. Multiple docs claim it is gitignored / must be built locally. Either the docs are wrong, or the binary should be removed from the index — a repo decision.


## High-severity findings (8)


### README.md — factual-drift
*unit: readme-root · location: "Named benchmarks (bench run)" section, line 72 (the code block headed "# Cartesian sweep: every benchmark × every index structure × every algorithm.") · finder severity: high → verified: high*

- **Claim:** `kermit bench run --all -i all -a all` — presented as the canonical "Cartesian sweep: every benchmark × every index structure × every algorithm."
- **Reality:** This command panics. `bench run` expands `-i all`/`-a all` into a full cross product and dispatches every (ds, algo) pair without filtering for compatibility. Incompatible pairs such as (TreeTrie, HashTriejoin) reach `instantiate_database`, which panics ("incompatible pair: ... HashTriejoin can only be used with HashTrie"). The top-level `supports_algorithm` gate only validates the selector pair, not the expanded concrete pairs, and is permissive whenever either side is `All`.
- **Evidence:** kermit/src/main.rs:1547-1608 (cross-product loop has no compatibility filter — only ds is matched, algo is passed straight through); kermit/src/db.rs:312-317 (`(TreeTrie | ColumnTrie, HashTriejoin) => panic!`) and kermit/src/db.rs:308-311; kermit/src/main.rs:128-143 (`supports_algorithm` returns true whenever either side is `All`). Confirmed by the project's own commit 297baf0: "the expanded cross-product panics at kermit/src/db.rs:313 and writes no report ... The CLI panic itself is filed separately."
- **Suggested fix:** Replace the `--all -i all -a all` example with the set of valid configurations actually supported (one `bench run` per compatible pair), e.g. `-i tree-trie -a leapfrog-triejoin`, `-i column-trie -a leapfrog-triejoin`, `-i hash-trie -a hash-triejoin`. Do not advertise `-i all -a all` as a working sweep until the cross-product loop filters incompatible pairs.
- **Verifier note:** CONFIRMED. README.md:71-72 advertises `kermit bench run --all -i all -a all` as the canonical "Cartesian sweep: every benchmark × every index structure × every algorithm." This command panics.  Verified chain in code: 1. `IndexStructureSelector::All.expand()` → `[TreeTrie, ColumnTrie, HashTrie]` (kermit/src/main.rs:112); `JoinAlgorithmSelector::All.expand()` → `[LeapfrogTriejoin, HashTriejoin]` (main.rs:163). So `-i all -a all` produces all 6 concrete pairs, including incompatible ones. 2. The cross-product loop (main.rs:1547-1608) iterates `benchmark × ds × algo` and dispatches with `algo` passed straight through; the `match ds` branches only on index structure and contains NO compatibility filter / `continue`. The inline comment at main.rs:1527-1528 ("the cross-product loop below already filters individual concrete pairs at dispatch time") and the comment at main.rs:124-127 are FALSE — no such filtering exists. 3. For `(TreeTrie, HashTriejoin)`, `run_benchmark::<TreeTrie>` calls `instantiate_database(TreeTrie, HashTriejoin, …)` (main.rs:921), which hits the arm at db.rs:312-317: `(TreeTrie | ColumnTrie, HashTriejoin) => panic!("incompatible pair: … HashTriejoin can only be used with HashTrie …")`. 4. The upstream guard `supports_algorithm` (main.rs:128-143) returns `true` whenever either side is `All` (line 132), so the `anyhow::bail!` at main.rs:1529-1535 does NOT reject `-i all -a all`. It validates only the selector pair, not the expansion.  Corroborated by the project's own commit 297baf0 ("the expanded cross-product panics at kermit/src/db.rs:313 and writes no report"; "The CLI panic itself is filed separately") and the user-memory note project_bench_run_all_all_panics.md. The finder's evidence is accurate and its suggested fix (enumerate the 3 valid configs: tree-trie/column-trie + leapfrog-triejoin, hash-trie + hash-triejoin) matches the remediation the project itself adopted.  Severity: high is correct. This is the top-level README's primary advertised sweep command; a reader copy-pasting it gets an immediate runtime panic and no report written (the loop hits TreeTrie+HashTriejoin and aborts before write_bench_report at main.rs:1610). It is a genuine user-facing doc-vs-code discrepancy, but not critical (no data loss/security), so high — not medium (it is the headline example), not critical.

### USAGE.md — stale-content
*unit: usage · location: Section 'Sweep all index structures and algorithms', lines 170-172 (the `bench run --all -i all -a all` example) · finder severity: high → verified: high*

- **Claim:** # Full Cartesian sweep: every benchmark x every DS x every algorithm. kermit bench run --all -i all -a all
- **Reality:** `bench run -i all -a all` panics on incompatible (index-structure, algorithm) pairs. The `supports_algorithm` gate is permissive when either side is `All`, and the cross-product loop does NOT skip incompatible concrete pairs. For pair `(TreeTrie, HashTriejoin)` (and `(ColumnTrie, HashTriejoin)`), `run_benchmark::<TreeTrie>` calls `instantiate_database(TreeTrie, HashTriejoin)`, whose match arm panics: 'incompatible pair: (TreeTrie, HashTriejoin)'. Presenting this command as a clean full sweep is misleading; it aborts mid-run.
- **Evidence:** kermit/src/main.rs:1529-1535 gates with `supports_algorithm` which returns true for any `All` (main.rs:132); the loop at kermit/src/main.rs:1547-1609 iterates every (ds, algo) without filtering and dispatches `(TreeTrie, algo)` to `run_benchmark::<TreeTrie>`; run_benchmark calls `instantiate_database(indexstructure, algorithm, ...)` at main.rs:921; db.rs:312-317 panics on `(TreeTrie | ColumnTrie, HashTriejoin)`. This matches the project memory note 'bench run -i all -a all panics' and recent commit 297baf0 correcting the same false claim elsewhere.
- **Suggested fix:** Drop the `-i all -a all` example or replace it with the three valid configurations enumerated explicitly: `-i tree-trie -a leapfrog-triejoin`, `-i column-trie -a leapfrog-triejoin`, `-i hash-trie -a hash-triejoin`. If `-i all -a all` is shown at all, add an explicit warning that it currently panics on incompatible pairs.
- **Verifier note:** USAGE.md:170-171 documents `kermit bench run --all -i all -a all` as a clean "Full Cartesian sweep: every benchmark × every DS × every algorithm" with no caveat (line 174 reinforces the sweep framing). In reality this command panics on the first iteration. Trace: the up-front gate at kermit/src/main.rs:1529 uses supports_algorithm, which returns true whenever either side is All (main.rs:132), so `-i all -a all` is accepted. The cross-product loop at main.rs:1547-1609 contains NO filtering/continue — its ColumnTrie/TreeTrie arms (1551, 1559) call run_benchmark::<…> passing every `algo` through unconditionally. run_benchmark then calls instantiate_database(indexstructure, algorithm, …) at main.rs:921, and instantiate_database panics on `(TreeTrie | ColumnTrie, HashTriejoin)` at db.rs:312-317. Enum orders (IndexStructure=[ColumnTrie,HashTrie,TreeTrie] at kermit-ds/src/ds/mod.rs:12-19; JoinAlgorithm=[HashTriejoin,LeapfrogTriejoin] at kermit-algos/src/lib.rs:36-42) make the first loop pair `(ColumnTrie, HashTriejoin)` panic immediately — even before `(TreeTrie, HashTriejoin)`. The report write at main.rs:1610 is after the loop, so no report is produced. Corroboration: commit 297baf0 corrected this exact false claim ("-i all -a all skips incompatible pairs") in the specs/plans citing db.rs:313, and the project memory note documents the identical trace. The misleading inline comments at main.rs:1526-1528 and 130-131 ("the cross-product loop below already filters individual concrete pairs") are themselves false. Minor: the finder names `(TreeTrie, HashTriejoin)` as the panicking pair; the actual first one is `(ColumnTrie, HashTriejoin)`, but both hit the same arm and the verdict is unaffected. Severity high concurs with the finder: it is a user-facing USAGE.md command presented as the go-to full sweep that hard-panics with zero output; a near-identical claim already warranted a correction commit. Suggested fix (enumerate the 3 valid configs, or add a panic warning) is appropriate.

### docs/benchmarks/WATDIV.md — factual-drift
*unit: bench-watdiv · location: Vendored generator section, lines 253 and 263-267 · finder severity: critical → verified: high*

- **Claim:** `kermit-rdf/vendor/watdiv/bin/Release/watdiv` (~448 KB, **gitignored** — build it locally; not committed to the repo). ... The binary itself is excluded from the repository (commit `1aaac0d`); ... Builders must compile the binary locally and place it under `kermit-rdf/vendor/watdiv/bin/Release/watdiv` before the on-the-fly pipeline will run.
- **Reality:** The watdiv binary IS committed to the repository. `git cat-file -t HEAD:kermit-rdf/vendor/watdiv/bin/Release/watdiv` returns `blob` (size 360112), and `git ls-files` lists the path as tracked. The binary was committed in ca0815c ('feat(kermit-rdf): vendor watdiv binary + support files'). Commit 1aaac0d ('chore: add watdiv binary to .gitignore') only added a single line to .gitignore — it did not remove the already-tracked binary, and git ignore rules have no effect on files already under version control. `git check-ignore` returns empty for the path. Builders therefore do NOT need to compile it locally; it ships with the repo.
- **Evidence:** git cat-file -t HEAD:kermit-rdf/vendor/watdiv/bin/Release/watdiv => blob; git cat-file -s HEAD:kermit-rdf/vendor/watdiv/bin/Release/watdiv => 360112; git log --oneline -- kermit-rdf/vendor/watdiv/bin/Release/watdiv => ca0815c feat(kermit-rdf): vendor watdiv binary + support files; git show --stat 1aaac0d => only `.gitignore | 1 +`
- **Suggested fix:** Rewrite the section to state the binary is committed (vendored, ~360 KB) and present at kermit-rdf/vendor/watdiv/bin/Release/watdiv; remove the 'build it locally / not committed / Builders must compile' language. If the intent is to STOP committing it, that is a code/repo change, not a doc edit. Note CLAUDE.md ('binary at bin/Release/watdiv is gitignored, build locally') is wrong in the same way and should be reconciled.
- **Verifier note:** The doc genuinely misrepresents repo reality. docs/benchmarks/WATDIV.md:253 states the binary is "(~448 KB, **gitignored** — build it locally; not committed to the repo)" and lines 262-267 claim "The binary itself is excluded from the repository (commit `1aaac0d`)" and "Builders must compile the binary locally ... before the on-the-fly pipeline will run." All false. The binary IS committed and tracked: `git cat-file -t HEAD:kermit-rdf/vendor/watdiv/bin/Release/watdiv` => blob (360112 bytes); `git ls-tree HEAD` => `100755 blob e3752e9`; `git ls-files` lists it. It is NOT ignored: `git check-ignore -v` returns empty (exit 1) because ignore rules are inert on already-tracked files — the `.gitignore:35 **/watdiv` pattern has no effect here. HEAD blob sha256 equals on-disk sha256 (519c4a9...), so the committed bytes are the working binary and present in a fresh clone. `git log` for the path shows only ca0815c (vendoring commit); 1aaac0d changed only `.gitignore | 1 +` and removed nothing. The cited size is also wrong (actual ~360 KB, not 448 KB). A reader would be misled into an unnecessary local build and into believing the binary is absent from a clone. Not critical (no data corruption / silent-wrong-result / build break), but a clear load-bearing setup-prerequisite error = high. Note CLAUDE.md:77 carries the identical false 'gitignored, build locally' claim and should be reconciled (out of the doc's audit scope but corroborates the drift).

### docs/data-structures/hash-trie.md — factual-drift
*unit: doc-inventory · location: ## Invariants, second bullet 'Hash function consistency' (line 38) · finder severity: high → verified: high*

- **Claim:** "`hash_attribute(depth, key)` is the single source of truth for the hashing convention, exported from `kermit-iters`. It ignores the `depth` parameter and hashes only the key value. The depth parameter is preserved in the signature for ABI compatibility with the original design but does not affect the output. ... Both `HashTrie::insert_at` and `SingletonHashTrieIter::new` import this function; any divergence silently breaks queries against constants."
- **Reality:** No function named `hash_attribute` exists anywhere in the repository (repo-wide grep returns zero matches). Hashing is done through the `HashStrategy` trait, whose method is `fn hash(key: usize) -> u64` — a single key argument with NO `depth` parameter. `HashTrie::insert_at` computes `let hash = H::hash(key);` and `SingletonHashTrieIter::new(value, hash)` takes a precomputed hash from `H::hash`. The `kermit-iters` export is `HashStrategy` (with `SipHashStrategy`/`FxHashStrategy`), not `hash_attribute`.
- **Evidence:** kermit-iters/src/hash_strategy.rs:13-16 (`pub trait HashStrategy { fn hash(key: usize) -> u64; }`); kermit-ds/src/ds/hash_trie/implementation.rs:97 (`let hash = H::hash(key);`); kermit-algos/src/hash_singleton.rs:51 (`pub fn new(value: usize, hash: u64)`) and hash_singleton.rs:143 (`fn h(value: usize) -> u64 { SipHashStrategy::hash(value) }`); repo-wide `grep -rn hash_attribute --include=*.rs` returns nothing.
- **Suggested fix:** Rewrite the invariant to describe the real mechanism: hashing flows through the `HashStrategy` trait's single-argument `fn hash(key: usize) -> u64` (exported from `kermit-iters` as `HashStrategy`, default `SipHashStrategy`). Remove the `hash_attribute(depth, key)` signature, the 'exported from kermit-iters' / 'imports this function' claims, and the 'depth parameter preserved for ABI compatibility' narrative, since no such function exists.
- **Verifier note:** CONFIRMED. docs/data-structures/hash-trie.md:38 ("Hash function consistency" invariant) describes a function `hash_attribute(depth, key)` exported from kermit-iters, with a depth parameter "preserved for ABI compatibility," imported by both HashTrie::insert_at and SingletonHashTrieIter::new. No such function exists in any Rust source: `grep -rn hash_attribute` over the repo returns matches ONLY in docs/plans/ and docs/specs/ (historical design docs), never in *.rs. The real mechanism is the HashStrategy trait at kermit-iters/src/hash_strategy.rs:13-16: `pub trait HashStrategy { fn hash(key: usize) -> u64; }` — single key arg, NO depth param. kermit-ds/src/ds/hash_trie/implementation.rs:97 does `let hash = H::hash(key);`. kermit-algos/src/hash_singleton.rs:51 is `pub fn new(value: usize, hash: u64)` taking a precomputed hash, with its doc comment (lines 45-50) explicitly pointing at kermit_iters::HashStrategy. The doc is even self-contradictory: its own Optimizations section (hash-trie.md:106-114) correctly documents `HashTrie<H: HashStrategy>` with SipHashStrategy/FxHashStrategy in kermit_iters::hash_strategy. Git history confirms hash_attribute was deliberately removed (docs/specs/2026-05-27-optimization-standard-design.md:204, docs/plans/2026-05-27-optimization-standard-plan.md:50) and the invariant bullet was never updated. Severity high is appropriate: the bullet fabricates a function signature plus a false ABI-compatibility rationale for a load-bearing data-structure invariant that itself warns it "silently breaks queries against constants" — a maintainer following the CLAUDE.md extension recipe would search for the nonexistent function and be misled about the hashing contract.

### docs/specs/space-benchmarks.md — factual-drift
*unit: spec-space · location: Benchmark ID Mapping section (lines 225-247); also pervasive in Directory Layout (lines 9-30), benchmark.json example (lines 39-50), and throughout the examples · finder severity: critical → verified: high*

- **Claim:** "The benchmark function ID `Space/{Generator}/{param}/{n}` maps to the filesystem as `Space_{Generator}_{param}_{n}`." with a table of 14 IDs such as `Space/Exponential/3/27` and `Space/Factorial/9/362880`, and the benchmark.json example showing `"function_id": "Space/Exponential/3/27"`, `"full_id": "TreeTrie/Space/Exponential/3/27"`, `"directory_name": "TreeTrie/Space_Exponential_3_27"`.
- **Reality:** No such function IDs are produced anywhere in the workspace. The implemented space benchmark produces a single function ID `"{ds_name}/space"` per data structure (e.g. `TreeTrie/space`). There is no Exponential/Factorial parameterisation in the code: no `kermit-ds/benches/` directory, no `space_benchmarks.rs` bench binary, no `tuple_generation` module, and no `generate_exponential_tuples`/`generate_factorial_tuples` functions exist (verified by repo-wide grep — they appear only in `docs/plans/*`). The single `[[bench]]` target in the workspace (kermit-algos/Cargo.toml:19-21) is commented out.
- **Evidence:** kermit/src/main.rs:727 `let function = format!("{ds_name}/space");` (also kermit/src/main.rs:846 for the hash path); kermit/src/main.rs:666 `let ds_name = format!("{:?}", indexstructure);`; kermit/src/main.rs:722-731 (space block in run_ds_bench). Repo grep: no `*.rs` file matches `Exponential|Factorial|generate_exponential_tuples|generate_factorial_tuples`; `find` finds no `benches/` dir and no `space_benchmarks.rs`; kermit-algos/Cargo.toml:19-21 shows the only `[[bench]]` is commented out. The Exponential/Factorial naming and the `kermit-ds/benches/space_benchmarks.rs` binary appear only in the never-implemented plan docs/plans/2026-03-16-space-benchmarks-design.md:45-52 and docs/plans/2026-03-16-space-benchmarks-impl.md:152,168.
- **Suggested fix:** Replace the entire 'Benchmark ID Mapping' table and all `Space/Exponential|Factorial/...` examples with the actual scheme: function ID is `<DataStructure>/space` (e.g. `TreeTrie/space`), produced by `kermit bench ds <relation> -i <ds> --metrics space` and `kermit bench run`. Update the benchmark.json example to `{"group_id":"TreeTrie","function_id":"TreeTrie/space","directory_name":"TreeTrie/TreeTrie_space", ...}` consistent with the code. Remove the Exponential/Factorial parameter dimension entirely (it was planned but never built / superseded by the bench-ds CLI path).
- **Verifier note:** CONFIRMED. docs/specs/space-benchmarks.md does say what is claimed: lines 29-30 ("Space/Exponential/3/27 → Space_Exponential_3_27"), lines 42/47-49 (benchmark.json with function_id "Space/Exponential/3/27", full_id "TreeTrie/Space/Exponential/3/27", directory_name "TreeTrie/Space_Exponential_3_27"), and the 14-row Benchmark ID Mapping table at lines 225-247 (Space/Exponential/* and Space/Factorial/*). The actual implemented scheme is a single function ID per data structure: kermit/src/main.rs:727 and :846 both `let function = format!("{ds_name}/space")`, with ds_name = format!("{:?}", indexstructure) at main.rs:666 — yielding e.g. TreeTrie/space. add_space_bench (main.rs:615-642) registers exactly that one string; no per-k subtasks. The code's own unit test agrees: kermit/src/bench_report.rs:227,243 assert "TreeTrie/space". Independently verified negatives: repo-wide grep across *.rs for Exponential|Factorial|generate_exponential_tuples|generate_factorial_tuples|tuple_generation returns no matches (exit 1); find shows no benches/ dir and no space_benchmarks.rs outside target/; the only [[bench]] target (kermit-algos/Cargo.toml:19-21) is commented out. The Exponential/Factorial naming and benches/space_benchmarks.rs appear ONLY in plan docs — though the surviving sources are docs/plans/2026-03-17-benchmarking-consolidation-{design,impl}.md (e.g. design.md:71-72,122-145; impl.md:43,71), not the 2026-03-16 files the finder cited (minor citation slip, does not affect verdict). The entire Benchmark ID Mapping table, the Directory Layout example (line 12), and the benchmark.json example are fabricated and would misdirect anyone parsing target/criterion output or benchmark.json. Severity adjusted critical→high: it is a spec doc and the error is pervasive/load-bearing, but it is documentation-only describing benchmark output paths (no build/correctness/security impact) and the real scheme is trivially discoverable (CLAUDE.md JSON-bench-reports gotcha already documents function = {ds}/space and directory_name sourced from benchmark.json). Suggested fix is correct: replace the table/examples with the {DataStructure}/space scheme and remove the Exponential/Factorial dimension.

### docs/specs/space-benchmarks.md — factual-drift
*unit: spec-space · location: Directory Layout, lines 11-12 and 27; Benchmark ID Mapping closing line 247 · finder severity: high → verified: high*

- **Claim:** "**Group** is the data structure name (`TreeTrie`, `ColumnTrie`)." and "Groups: `TreeTrie`, `ColumnTrie` (28 benchmark directories total)."
- **Reality:** The Criterion group name is `format!("{:?}", indexstructure)` over the `IndexStructure` enum, whose variants are `ColumnTrie`, `HashTrie`, and `TreeTrie`. HashTrie also runs space benchmarks (via the dedicated `run_ds_bench_hash` path), so the set of possible groups is three, not the two listed. The doc omits `HashTrie` entirely. The '28 benchmark directories total' count is a function of the non-existent 14-ID Exponential/Factorial matrix x 2 groups and is therefore also wrong.
- **Evidence:** kermit-ds/src/ds/mod.rs:12-19 `pub enum IndexStructure { ColumnTrie, HashTrie, TreeTrie }`; kermit/src/main.rs:785 `let ds_name = format!("{:?}", indexstructure);` inside `run_ds_bench_hash`; kermit/src/main.rs:841-850 adds the `{ds_name}/space` bench for the hash family. There is one space function per data structure, not 14, so '28 ... total' has no basis in the code.
- **Suggested fix:** State that the group name is the `IndexStructure` Debug string, one of `ColumnTrie`, `HashTrie`, `TreeTrie`. Remove the '(28 benchmark directories total)' count, or replace with the correct figure (one `<DS>/space` function per data structure benchmarked).
- **Verifier note:** Doc claims confirmed verbatim: docs/specs/space-benchmarks.md:27 says "Group is the data structure name (TreeTrie, ColumnTrie)" and line 247 says "Groups: TreeTrie, ColumnTrie (28 benchmark directories total)". Code reality confirms the finding: kermit-ds/src/ds/mod.rs:12-19 defines IndexStructure { ColumnTrie, HashTrie, TreeTrie } — three variants. HashTrie genuinely runs space benchmarks: run_ds_bench_hash (kermit/src/main.rs:766) builds a {ds_name}/space function at main.rs:846 (ds_name = format!("{:?}", indexstructure), main.rs:785), and the Ds dispatch routes HashTrie there at main.rs:1492-1507. So the doc omits a real third group/identity. The '28 = 14 IDs × 2 groups' count is doubly baseless: a grep for Exponential/Factorial/"Space/" across all .rs files returns nothing, so the entire Space/{Generator}/{param}/{n} ID matrix (lines 12, 30, 230-245) does not exist in the current code — there is exactly one {ds_name}/space function per benchmarked structure (grep count = 2 definitions, one generic + one hash). Tests pin the real shape: kermit/tests/cli_join_tests.rs:423 asserts function == "TreeTrie/space" and main.rs:1466/:54 show the Criterion group is actually "ds" (DEFAULT_DS_GROUP) / bench_args.name, with the DS Debug string used as the function prefix — so even the group-vs-function model in the doc is wrong, though that nuance only reinforces the finding. Doc was authored 2026-03-17 (commit 400d931) and never updated. Finder's core claim and high severity are correct; the only minor imprecision in the finder's wording is calling the DS-name the 'group' rather than the function prefix, which does not affect the verdict.

### docs/specs/space-benchmarks.md — stale-content
*unit: spec-space · location: Header/metadata, lines 1-3 (no usage/CLI context anywhere in the doc) · finder severity: high → verified: high*

- **Claim:** The doc is titled 'Space Benchmarks — Criterion Output Specification' (dated 2026-03-16) and frames space benchmarks as output of a standalone Criterion benchmark binary with Exponential/Factorial generators, with no mention of the `kermit bench ds`/`bench run --metrics space` CLI path that actually produces the output.
- **Reality:** Space benchmarking is implemented entirely in the CLI (`kermit bench ds ... --metrics space` and `kermit bench run ... --metrics space`), routed through `kermit::measurement::SpaceMeasurement`. The `heap_size.rs` doc-comment itself points at this CLI path, and a later consolidation explicitly replaced the planned `kermit-ds` Exponential/Factorial benchmarks. The spec was written against the pre-consolidation design and was never updated.
- **Evidence:** kermit-ds/src/heap_size.rs:4-6 doc comment: 'Used by the space benchmarks (`bench ds --metrics space` / `bench run --metrics space`) routed through `kermit::measurement::SpaceMeasurement`.'; kermit/src/main.rs:722-731 and 841-850 (space metric handled in bench ds path); docs/plans/2026-03-17-benchmarking-consolidation-design.md:58-59,115,134 ('replaces kermit-ds exponential/factorial benchmarks'). The consolidation removed the binary this spec documents.
- **Suggested fix:** Reframe the doc around the actual CLI surface (`kermit bench ds <relation> -i <ds> --metrics space`), and either delete the Exponential/Factorial generator framing or move it to a 'historical / superseded' note. Cross-link the live spec `docs/specs/bench-report-schema.md`.
- **Verifier note:** Confirmed and stronger than claimed. docs/specs/space-benchmarks.md:1-3 is titled "Space Benchmarks — Criterion Output Specification" (dated 2026-03-16) and frames output as coming from a standalone Criterion binary with Exponential/Factorial generators; lines 12,29-30,47-49,225-247 build an entire "Benchmark ID Mapping" table of `Space/Exponential/{param}/{n}` → `Space_Exponential_*` directories (28 rows). The doc never mentions the CLI; grep for `bench ds|bench run|--metrics` returns only line 66's incidental `SpaceMeasurement::Value` reference. Reality matches the finder: space benchmarking is CLI-only — kermit/src/main.rs:722-731 (run_ds_bench) and 841-850 (run_ds_bench_hash) handle `Metric::Space`, producing function id `{ds_name}/space` (lines 727,846) via add_space_bench (main.rs:615-642) routed through measurement::SpaceMeasurement. kermit-ds/src/heap_size.rs:4-6 verbatim points at `bench ds --metrics space` / `bench run --metrics space`. The Exponential/Factorial benches were removed: a repo-wide find for exponential/factorial *.rs returns nothing, kermit-bench/src/ contains no generation/ or benchmarks/ dirs (only cache/definition/discovery/error/lib.rs), and kermit-ds/benches/ does not exist; the consolidation plan docs/plans/2026-03-17-benchmarking-consolidation-design.md:58-59,115,134 documents their replacement. The live sibling spec bench-report-schema.md:38 already records the real identity `{"group":"ds","function":"TreeTrie/space","metric":"space"}`, directly contradicting this doc's mapping table. The stale doc is cross-linked as the authority on space measurement from BENCHMARKING.md:63,200, so a reader is actively misdirected toward a nonexistent generator binary and nonexistent directory names. High is appropriate: wrong production mechanism, wrong function-id scheme, and a 28-row table mapping to directories that can never be produced. (JSON-shape sections for estimates/sample/tukey remain accurate, so not fully fabricated.)

### kermit-bench/src/definition.rs — factual-drift
*unit: rust-bench · location: Doc comment on `BenchmarkDefinition::validate`, lines 167-176 · finder severity: high → verified: high*

- **Claim:** "Validates structural invariants of the benchmark definition. Checks that `name`, `relations`, and `queries` are non-empty, that every query has a non-empty `name` and `query`, and that relation names and query names are unique within the benchmark."
- **Reality:** The doc describes pre-generator behaviour. (1) It is wrong that `relations` and `queries` must be non-empty: for a generator benchmark the code takes the `(Some(spec), false)` arm and delegates to `validate_generator` (definition.rs:237), and the XOR (definition.rs:221-239) actually *requires* relations/queries to be empty when a `generator` is present. (2) The doc omits two structural invariants that `validate` now enforces up front: the name-portability check rejecting path separators / `.` / `..` (definition.rs:212-219, via `is_portable_filename`) and the generator-vs-static XOR (definition.rs:221-239). The struct-level doc (definition.rs:9-14) and the `Some(_), true` error message "cannot mix `generator` with `relations`/`queries`" both confirm generators are a first-class, validated case that this function's doc ignores.
- **Evidence:** kermit-bench/src/definition.rs:199-291 (full `validate` body); name-portability check at definition.rs:212-219; generator/static XOR at definition.rs:221-239; generator delegation at definition.rs:237 -> `validate_generator` (definition.rs:294-362); struct doc stating the XOR at definition.rs:9-14
- **Suggested fix:** Rewrite the summary to cover both cases, e.g.: "Validates structural invariants. Requires the benchmark to declare *either* `relations`+`queries` *or* a `generator` (the two are mutually exclusive). `name` must be non-empty and a portable filename (ASCII alphanumerics plus `.`/`_`/`-`, no path separators or `..`). For static benchmarks, `relations` and `queries` must be non-empty, every query must have a non-empty `name`/`query`, and relation and query names must be unique. For generator benchmarks, the spec is validated by `validate_generator` (scale >= 1, etc.)."
- **Verifier note:** The doc comment at kermit-bench/src/definition.rs:167-176 says validate "Checks that `name`, `relations`, and `queries` are non-empty, that every query has a non-empty `name` and `query`, and that relation names and query names are unique." This describes only the static-benchmark path and materially misrepresents the actual function (verified by reading the full body at definition.rs:199-291).  (1) The "relations and queries are non-empty" claim is not just incomplete but inverted for generator benchmarks. definition.rs:221-239 implements an XOR: has_static = !relations.is_empty() || !queries.is_empty() (line 221); the (Some(_), true) arm at line 223 rejects "cannot mix `generator` with `relations`/`queries`". So when a generator is present, relations/queries must be EMPTY, and control delegates to validate_generator via the (Some(spec), false) arm at line 237. The non-emptiness checks at lines 241-253 are only reached on the static (None, true) path.  (2) The doc omits the name-portability invariant at definition.rs:212-219, which rejects names containing path separators / `.` / `..` / non-[alnum._-] chars via is_portable_filename (definition.rs:381-387). This is a real reject path guarding fs::remove_dir_all from escaping the cache subdir (comment at lines 207-211); the doc only says `name` must be "non-empty".  (3) The doc gives no hint that a generator is a validated case at all, despite the struct-level doc at definition.rs:9-14 explicitly naming validate as the enforcer of the generator/static XOR — so the two docs in the same file contradict each other.  This is genuine factual drift, not a misread or stylistic nit: a caller relying on the docstring would write wrong code/tests for generator benchmarks and would not anticipate the portability reject. Severity high is appropriate (one invariant is inverted for a first-class case), though bounded by the fact that the validation logic itself is correct and the struct doc already documents the XOR. The finder's suggested rewrite accurately captures both branches.

## Medium-severity findings (41)


### ARCHITECTURE.md — factual-drift
*unit: architecture · location: Core Abstractions → Data Structures → Relation Trait (lines 99-105, code block) · finder severity: high → verified: medium*

- **Claim:** The Relation trait is shown as: ```rust pub trait Relation: JoinIterable + Projectable {     fn header(&self) -> &RelationHeader;     fn new(header: RelationHeader) -> Self;     fn from_tuples(header: RelationHeader, tuples: Vec<Vec<usize>>) -> Self;     fn insert(&mut self, tuple: Vec<usize>) -> bool;     fn insert_all(&mut self, tuples: Vec<Vec<usize>>) -> bool; } ```
- **Reality:** The actual `Relation` trait declares `insert` and `insert_all` as returning unit (`()`), not `bool`. Duplicate tuples are silently absorbed; there is no boolean success/insertion result.
- **Evidence:** kermit-ds/src/relation.rs:261 `fn insert(&mut self, tuple: Vec<usize>);` and kermit-ds/src/relation.rs:270 `fn insert_all(&mut self, tuples: Vec<Vec<usize>>);` (both within `pub trait Relation` starting at relation.rs:237). The doc-comment at relation.rs:255-256 confirms 'Duplicate tuples are silently absorbed (the relation behaves as a set).' CLAUDE.md's Relation summary also lists `insert`/`insert_all` with no return value.
- **Suggested fix:** Remove the `-> bool` return types so both lines read `fn insert(&mut self, tuple: Vec<usize>);` and `fn insert_all(&mut self, tuples: Vec<Vec<usize>>);`.
- **Verifier note:** Confirmed real discrepancy. ARCHITECTURE.md:103-104 (inside the Relation-trait code block at lines 99-105) declares `fn insert(&mut self, tuple: Vec<usize>) -> bool;` and `fn insert_all(&mut self, tuples: Vec<Vec<usize>>) -> bool;`. The actual trait is the sole `pub trait Relation` in the workspace (kermit-ds/src/relation.rs:237) and declares both methods returning unit: relation.rs:261 `fn insert(&mut self, tuple: Vec<usize>);` and relation.rs:270 `fn insert_all(&mut self, tuples: Vec<Vec<usize>>);`. The doc-comment at relation.rs:255-256 states "Duplicate tuples are silently absorbed (the relation behaves as a set)", consistent with no boolean result. `grep -rn "pub trait Relation"` returns only relation.rs:237 (plus the separate `RelationFileExt` extension trait at :280), so there is no alternate trait the doc could be describing. CLAUDE.md's Relation summary also lists `insert`/`insert_all` with no return value. The fix (drop `-> bool` on both lines) is correct. Severity lowered from high to medium: this is an isolated return-type annotation drift on two methods (unit vs bool); the surrounding semantics are not misrepresented and any implementer would be caught immediately by the compiler, so blast radius is small.

### ARCHITECTURE.md — factual-drift
*unit: architecture · location: Workspace Structure → Dependency Graph (ASCII diagram, line 34) · finder severity: medium → verified: medium*

- **Claim:** The dependency graph draws `kermit-ds ◄─────── kermit-algos`, which (per the diagram's own arrow convention, e.g. line 29 `kermit-iters ◄─── kermit-derive`) means kermit-algos depends on kermit-ds.
- **Reality:** kermit-algos does NOT have a normal (production) dependency on kermit-ds. kermit-ds appears only under `[dev-dependencies]`, used by the algos crate's tests. The production algorithms are generic over `DS: TrieIterable` (a trait from kermit-iters), which is precisely Design Goal #1 'Algorithm-Data Structure Decoupling' stated at the top of this same doc. The diagram therefore contradicts the doc's stated architecture.
- **Evidence:** kermit-algos/Cargo.toml lines 12-15 list only `kermit-iters`, `kermit-derive`, `kermit-parser` as `[dependencies]`; `kermit-ds` is listed at kermit-algos/Cargo.toml:26 under `[dev-dependencies]`. The trait bound is visible at kermit-algos/src/leapfrog_triejoin.rs:448-451 `impl<DS> JoinAlgo<DS> for LeapfrogTriejoin where DS: TrieIterable`. (Note: the `kermit-iters ◄─── kermit-derive` edge on line 29 is similarly dev-only — kermit-iters is a dev-dependency at kermit-derive/Cargo.toml:21 — but that is the conventional way to depict a proc-macro crate.)
- **Suggested fix:** Either drop the `kermit-ds ◄─────── kermit-algos` edge from the graph, or annotate it (and the `kermit-derive`→`kermit-iters` edge) as a dev-dependency-only relationship so the diagram does not assert a runtime coupling that the decoupling design goal explicitly avoids.
- **Verifier note:** Confirmed real discrepancy. ARCHITECTURE.md:34 draws `kermit-ds ◄─────── kermit-algos`. The arrow convention `A ◄─── B` = "B depends on A" is established at ARCHITECTURE.md:29 and used consistently (e.g. line 37 kermit-rdf edge); the prose at line 44 restating production deps confirms the graph is meant as a literal crate-dependency graph. So the diagram asserts kermit-algos has a production dependency on kermit-ds. Reality: kermit-algos/Cargo.toml lists only kermit-iters/kermit-derive/kermit-parser under `[dependencies]` (lines 13-15); kermit-ds is dev-only at line 26 `[dev-dependencies]`. Verified independently: `cargo tree -p kermit-algos -e normal` returns NO kermit-ds (only appears with dev-deps); every `kermit_ds` import in src/ sits inside a `#[cfg(test)]` module (hash_triejoin.rs:284, trie_iter_kind.rs:107, hash_trie_iter_kind.rs:108, leapfrog_triejoin.rs:488 all precede their kermit_ds uses). The production impl is generic over the trait: leapfrog_triejoin.rs:448-451 `impl<DS> JoinAlgo<DS> for LeapfrogTriejoin where DS: TrieIterable`. This directly contradicts Design Goal #1 "Algorithm-Data Structure Decoupling" stated at ARCHITECTURE.md:7 in the same file. Finder's caveat that the kermit-derive→kermit-iters edge (kermit-derive/Cargo.toml:20-21, also dev-only) is the conventional proc-macro depiction is accurate and not self-defeating. Severity medium is well-calibrated: it is a single diagram edge (not a code/correctness bug) and line 44's prose does not list kermit-ds among algos' deps, giving an attentive reader a partial corrective; but the at-a-glance dependency diagram asserting a runtime coupling that the headline design goal 27 lines above explicitly disavows is more than a trivial typo. Suggested fix (drop the edge or annotate it dev-only) is appropriate.

### CLAUDE.md — factual-drift
*unit: claude-md · location: Section 'Workspace Architecture' > kermit-rdf entry (lines 76-78) · finder severity: medium → verified: medium*

- **Claim:** Vendor dirs: `vendor/lubm-uba/lubm-uba.jar` (committed, ~2.9 MB) and `vendor/watdiv/` (binary at `bin/Release/watdiv` is **gitignored**, build locally; surrounding `MODEL.txt`/`files/`/`VERSION` are committed).
- **Reality:** The watdiv binary at kermit-rdf/vendor/watdiv/bin/Release/watdiv IS committed to the repo, not gitignored/build-locally. It is tracked in git (git ls-files lists it), its git blob is 360112 bytes of real content, and it was added in commit ca0815c 'feat(kermit-rdf): vendor watdiv binary + support files' (2026-05-04). The `**/watdiv` rule in .gitignore would ignore it only if it were untracked, but it was force-committed, so the working state contradicts the doc. (The lubm-uba.jar ~2.9 MB part is correct: file is 2915542 bytes.)
- **Evidence:** `git ls-files | grep -E '/watdiv$'` => kermit-rdf/vendor/watdiv/bin/Release/watdiv; `git cat-file -s HEAD:kermit-rdf/vendor/watdiv/bin/Release/watdiv` => 360112; `git log --oneline -- kermit-rdf/vendor/watdiv/bin/Release/watdiv` => ca0815c feat(kermit-rdf): vendor watdiv binary + support files; .gitignore:35 `**/watdiv`. The materialize.rs:71-72 comment confirms this is the binary the doc means.
- **Suggested fix:** Replace 'binary at `bin/Release/watdiv` is **gitignored**, build locally' with a statement reflecting reality, e.g. 'binary at `bin/Release/watdiv` is committed (force-added past the `**/watdiv` ignore rule)'. If the intent is that it SHOULD be gitignored, the discrepancy is in the repo, not just the doc.
- **Verifier note:** CLAUDE.md:76-78 states the watdiv binary at `bin/Release/watdiv` "is **gitignored**, build locally". This contradicts the actual repo: the binary is tracked in git (`git ls-files` => kermit-rdf/vendor/watdiv/bin/Release/watdiv), has a 360112-byte blob at HEAD (matching the 360112-byte, mode 0755 working-tree file), and was committed in ca0815c "feat(kermit-rdf): vendor watdiv binary + support files". `git check-ignore -v <path>` returns NOT IGNORED (exit nonzero) for the tracked file; only `git check-ignore --no-index` shows .gitignore:35 `**/watdiv` WOULD match if it were untracked — so the ignore rule is inert because the file was force-committed. The finder's reality and evidence are fully accurate. The rest of the same sentence is correct: lubm-uba.jar HEAD blob is 2915542 bytes (~2.9 MB, committed), and MODEL.txt/files/VERSION are all tracked (git ls-files confirms). The error also internally contradicts CLAUDE.md:202, which refers to "the vendored `kermit-rdf/vendor/watdiv` binary" as present. A contributor following line 77 would needlessly build watdiv locally when it is already committed. Real, non-trivial factual drift in the canonical instructions file; medium severity is appropriate (misleads/wastes effort but does not break builds since the binary is present regardless).

### README.md — factual-drift
*unit: readme-root · location: Whole document — index-structure / algorithm coverage. Concrete instances: line 56 ("Both `tree-trie` and `column-trie` index structures are supported") and line 130 ("Supported index structures: `tree-trie`, `column-trie`.") · finder severity: high → verified: medium*

- **Claim:** Line 56: "Both `tree-trie` and `column-trie` index structures are supported". Line 130: "Supported index structures: `tree-trie`, `column-trie`." The README never mentions `hash-trie` or the `hash-triejoin` algorithm anywhere.
- **Reality:** There are three index structures and two join algorithms exposed on the CLI. `hash-trie` is a real `--indexstructure` value and `hash-triejoin` is a real `--algorithm` value; the pair (hash-trie, hash-triejoin) is a fully wired, working benchmark path via `bench run -i hash-trie -a hash-triejoin`. The README's enumerations are closed lists that omit the third structure and the second algorithm entirely.
- **Evidence:** kermit-ds/src/ds/mod.rs:11-19 (`#[derive(... ValueEnum)] pub enum IndexStructure { ColumnTrie, HashTrie, TreeTrie }`); kermit-algos/src/lib.rs:36-42 (`pub enum JoinAlgorithm { HashTriejoin, LeapfrogTriejoin }`); kermit/src/main.rs:100-106 (`IndexStructureSelector { All, ColumnTrie, HashTrie, TreeTrie }`); kermit/src/main.rs:1581-1604 (HashTrie routes through `run_benchmark_hash`); kermit/src/main.rs:128-143 ((HashTrie, HashTriejoin) is an accepted pair).
- **Suggested fix:** Document the third structure and second algorithm. For `bench run`, state that the supported pairings are (tree-trie | column-trie) with leapfrog-triejoin and hash-trie with hash-triejoin. Note that hash-trie is unsupported on `kermit join` / `bench join` (those panic in `instantiate_database`, db.rs:300), so the line 56/130 lists are only correct if scoped explicitly to those two subcommands.
- **Verifier note:** Doc claims verified verbatim: README.md:56 ("Both `tree-trie` and `column-trie` index structures are supported; `bench ds` and `bench run` also accept `all` to sweep every variant.") and README.md:130 ("Supported index structures: `tree-trie`, `column-trie`."). grep over README.md confirms the only "hash" hit is "spec_hash" (line 80) — `hash-trie`/`hash-triejoin` are never mentioned anywhere in the README.  Asserted reality confirmed against code: kermit-ds/src/ds/mod.rs:12-19 defines three structures (ColumnTrie, HashTrie, TreeTrie); kermit-algos/src/lib.rs:36-42 defines two algorithms (HashTriejoin, LeapfrogTriejoin). The (HashTrie, HashTriejoin) pair is a real, fully-wired CLI path on `bench run` (main.rs:1581 → run_benchmark_hash) and `bench ds` (main.rs:1492 → run_ds_bench_hash); supports_algorithm (main.rs:134) accepts it; db.rs:388-437 has passing hash_join tests.  The finder's scoping nuance is also accurate: `kermit join` (main.rs:550) and `bench join` (main.rs:1396) both route through load_query → instantiate_database, which panics on (HashTrie, HashTriejoin) at db.rs:300. So line 130, read strictly within its `bench join` section, is literally correct (hash-trie is unsupported there). But line 56 is genuinely misleading: it brings `bench ds`/`bench run` into scope ("also accept `all` to sweep every variant") where hash-trie IS a supported variant, yet the word "Both" closes the structure list to two. On `bench run -i all`, "every variant" is three structures, not two.  This is a real, substantive omission — an entire index structure (1 of 3) and an entire join algorithm (1 of 2) absent from the project's front-page README — not a misread or stylistic nit. Severity lowered from high to medium: it misleads by omission rather than mis-instructs (no documented command is broken; line 130 is technically accurate in its narrow scope, and the same gap is documented elsewhere e.g. USAGE.md:63), so it falls short of high while clearly exceeding low. Suggested fix stands: document hash-trie/hash-triejoin and state the valid pairings, scoping the closed lists to `kermit join`/`bench join` where they hold.

### README.md — factual-drift
*unit: readme-root · location: "Named benchmarks (bench run)" section, line 64 · finder severity: medium → verified: medium*

- **Claim:** "Some benchmarks are *declarative generators* (e.g. `watdiv-100`, `lubm-1`) — instead of pointing at downloadable files they declare a `generator:` block and `bench run` materialises the data on first invocation."
- **Reality:** No benchmark named `watdiv-100` or `lubm-1` exists in `benchmarks/`, and neither resolves via discovery (so `bench run watdiv-100` would error with "benchmark not found" unless that exact name was previously generated). The committed declarative-generator YAMLs are `watdiv-basic`, `watdiv-example`, `watdiv-stress-default`, `lubm-example`, and `lubm-reference`. (`bench gen watdiv --scale 100` produces names of the form `watdiv-stress-100-<tag>`, and `bench gen lubm --scale 1` produces `lubm-1-<tag>` — never bare `watdiv-100`/`lubm-1`.)
- **Evidence:** `ls benchmarks/` shows no watdiv-100.yml or lubm-1.yml; the only files with a `generator:` block are benchmarks/lubm-example.yml, benchmarks/lubm-reference.yml, benchmarks/watdiv-basic.yml, benchmarks/watdiv-example.yml, benchmarks/watdiv-stress-default.yml. Gen naming: kermit/src/main.rs:1627 (`format!("watdiv-stress-{scale}-{tag}")`) and kermit/src/main.rs:1701 (`format!("lubm-{scale}-{tag}")`). Resolution failure path: kermit/src/main.rs:1300-1305 (`load_benchmark`/`load_cached_benchmark` → "benchmark not found").
- **Suggested fix:** Use example names that actually exist as committed generator YAMLs, e.g. `watdiv-basic`, `lubm-reference`, or `watdiv-stress-default`.
- **Verifier note:** README.md:64 verbatim cites `watdiv-100` and `lubm-1` as example declarative-generator benchmarks that `bench run` materializes on first invocation. Both names are fabricated. (1) No `watdiv-100.yml` or `lubm-1.yml` exists; the only YAMLs with a `generator:` block are benchmarks/{lubm-example,lubm-reference,watdiv-basic,watdiv-example,watdiv-stress-default}.yml (verified via grep -l "generator:"). (2) Resolution is strict-by-filename: load_benchmark (kermit-bench/src/discovery.rs:26-55) opens `benchmarks/{name}.yml` and requires the inner `name:` to equal the stem; a miss returns BenchError::NotFound, and resolve_benchmarks (kermit/src/main.rs:1300-1305) then tries load_cached_benchmark and otherwise errors "benchmark not found: {name}". So `bench run watdiv-100` / `bench run lubm-1` fail unless a cache subdir of that exact name was pre-generated. (3) The gen naming scheme never produces those names: format!("watdiv-stress-{scale}-{tag}") at kermit/src/main.rs:1627 and format!("lubm-{scale}-{tag}") at main.rs:1701, so --scale 100 → watdiv-stress-100-<tag> and --scale 1 → lubm-1-<tag>, never bare. (4) The existing watdiv-stress-100-*.yml files are static (0 generator: blocks), so they don't rescue the claim. A reader copying these example names gets a hard "benchmark not found" error. Medium is appropriate (copy-pasteable but illustrative/parenthetical; the same README at line 89 shows the correct gen naming `watdiv-stress-10-dev`, and existing committed generator names like `watdiv-basic`/`lubm-reference` are the right substitutes).

### USAGE.md — factual-drift
*unit: usage · location: Section 'Sweep all index structures and algorithms', lines 174-176 · finder severity: high → verified: medium*

- **Claim:** `-a all` currently expands to the same set as `-a leapfrog-triejoin` (LFTJ is the only concrete algorithm); the selector is wired up so adding a new `JoinAlgorithm` variant automatically joins the sweep.
- **Reality:** There are TWO concrete join algorithms, not one: `HashTriejoin` and `LeapfrogTriejoin`. `JoinAlgorithmSelector::All.expand()` returns `JoinAlgorithm::value_variants()`, i.e. `[HashTriejoin, LeapfrogTriejoin]`, which is strictly larger than `[LeapfrogTriejoin]`. So `-a all` does NOT expand to the same set as `-a leapfrog-triejoin`.
- **Evidence:** kermit-algos/src/lib.rs:36-42 defines `enum JoinAlgorithm { HashTriejoin, LeapfrogTriejoin }`; kermit/src/main.rs:160-167 (`JoinAlgorithmSelector::expand`) maps `Self::All => JoinAlgorithm::value_variants().to_vec()`; the test at kermit/src/main.rs:2026-2033 pins `All.expand()` to both variants and 2040-2042 confirms `HashTriejoin` is a concrete variant.
- **Suggested fix:** Replace the parenthetical with the truth: `-a all` expands to both concrete algorithms `hash-triejoin` and `leapfrog-triejoin`. Note that the hash family only pairs with `-i hash-trie` and the sorted family (`tree-trie`/`column-trie`) only pairs with `leapfrog-triejoin` (see `IndexStructureSelector::supports_algorithm`).
- **Verifier note:** CONFIRMED. USAGE.md:174-176 literally states `-a all` "currently expands to the same set as `-a leapfrog-triejoin` (LFTJ is the only concrete algorithm)". This is false. kermit-algos/src/lib.rs:35-42 defines `enum JoinAlgorithm` with TWO concrete variants, `HashTriejoin` and `LeapfrogTriejoin`, both documented as real algorithms. kermit-algos/src/hash_triejoin.rs:249-251 confirms `HashTriejoin` is a real struct with `impl<DS> JoinAlgo<DS>` (not a placeholder). kermit/src/main.rs:160-167: `JoinAlgorithmSelector::All.expand()` returns `JoinAlgorithm::value_variants().to_vec()` = both variants, while `LeapfrogTriejoin.expand()` returns just `[LeapfrogTriejoin]`. Tests at main.rs:2026-2042 pin `All.expand()` to all value_variants and confirm `HashTriejoin` is its own concrete singleton. So `-a all` = `[HashTriejoin, LeapfrogTriejoin]`, strictly larger than `[LeapfrogTriejoin]`; the doc's parenthetical and its set-equality claim are both wrong. The finder's suggested fix is accurate, including the `supports_algorithm` pairing rules (verified at main.rs:128-143: hash-trie<->hash-triejoin, tree-trie/column-trie<->leapfrog-triejoin). Note the second clause of the doc sentence ("adding a new `JoinAlgorithm` variant automatically joins the sweep") IS correct per main.rs:146-167. Severity downgraded from high to medium: it is a genuine factual error that would mislead a CLI user into thinking `-a all` is redundant and missing HashTriejoin, but the blast radius is a single parenthetical in usage docs with no crash/correctness impact, and the surrounding mechanism description is accurate.

### USAGE.md — factual-drift
*unit: usage · location: Section 'Sweep all index structures and algorithms', line 167 (inline comment) · finder severity: medium → verified: medium*

- **Claim:** # Sweep both DS variants on a single benchmark + algorithm. kermit bench run triangle -i all -a leapfrog-triejoin
- **Reality:** `-i all` expands to THREE index structures, not two: ColumnTrie, HashTrie, TreeTrie. The phrase 'both DS variants' undercounts. (With `-a leapfrog-triejoin` the run also reaches the `(HashTrie, LeapfrogTriejoin)` arm, which dispatches through `run_benchmark_hash` and silently runs hash-triejoin while labelling the report's algorithm axis as LeapfrogTriejoin — a separate correctness concern, but at minimum the count of 'both' is wrong.)
- **Evidence:** kermit-ds/src/ds/mod.rs:11-19 defines `enum IndexStructure { ColumnTrie, HashTrie, TreeTrie }`; kermit/src/main.rs:109-117 (`IndexStructureSelector::expand`) maps `Self::All => IndexStructure::value_variants().to_vec()`; test at kermit/src/main.rs:2004-2011 pins `All.expand()` to every variant (three).
- **Suggested fix:** Change 'both DS variants' to 'all three DS variants', or scope the example to the sorted-family structures explicitly (`-i tree-trie`/`-i column-trie`) since `-i all -a leapfrog-triejoin` also touches hash-trie.
- **Verifier note:** The doc claim is real: USAGE.md:167 says "# Sweep both DS variants on a single benchmark + algorithm." above `kermit bench run triangle -i all -a leapfrog-triejoin`. The code contradicts "both" (two). kermit-ds/src/ds/mod.rs:11-19 defines `enum IndexStructure { ColumnTrie, HashTrie, TreeTrie }` (three variants). kermit/src/main.rs:112 maps `IndexStructureSelector::All => IndexStructure::value_variants().to_vec()`, and the test at kermit/src/main.rs:2005-2011 pins `All.expand()` to all variants. So `-i all` sweeps three structures, not two — "both" is a factual undercount and presupposes exactly two. The finder's "separate correctness concern" also holds and reinforces the impact: the `bench run` cross-product loop (kermit/src/main.rs:1547-1608) dispatches purely on `ds` with NO per-pair compatibility filter, so with `-a leapfrog-triejoin` the `(HashTrie, LeapfrogTriejoin)` pair reaches the HashTrie arm (line 1581) and calls `run_benchmark_hash`, which executes hash_join but labels the report's algorithm axis from the passed-in `algorithm` value (lines 1117, 1137) — i.e. emits a run labelled "LeapfrogTriejoin" that actually ran hash-triejoin. The in-code comments at lines 1527-1528 and 1573-1575 falsely assert this pair is filtered out, but it isn't (consistent with the project_bench_run_all_all memory). A reader copying this exact example gets a 3-way sweep plus a mislabelled hash-trie row, so the example is actively misleading for benchmarking, not merely a wording nit. Medium is appropriate.

### USAGE.md — internal-inconsistency
*unit: usage · location: Section 'Pick the index structure', lines 63-65 · finder severity: medium → verified: medium*

- **Claim:** `--indexstructure` accepts `tree-trie` (pointer-based) or `column-trie` (column-oriented). Both implement the same `Relation` + `TrieIterable` traits and are interchangeable from the CLI's perspective; benchmark to pick one.
- **Reality:** A third index structure, `hash-trie`, exists and is a valid `--indexstructure` value for `bench ds`/`bench run`. The doc never mentions it here, yet later (line 340) it documents `ablation --axis ds_layout_hasher` and (line 349-357) the `ds_*` optimization axes — both of which only exist for hash-trie. The result is an internally inconsistent picture: the usage section claims two interchangeable structures while the analysis section assumes a hash-trie axis. (For the bare `kermit join` command hash-trie does panic via instantiate_database, so its omission there is defensible, but the doc should say so rather than imply only two structures exist system-wide.)
- **Evidence:** kermit-ds/src/ds/mod.rs:11-19 defines three variants including `HashTrie`; kermit/src/main.rs:101-106 (`IndexStructureSelector`) and 183-190 (`HasherChoice`) expose `hash-trie` plus the `--ds-layout-hasher` flag; USAGE.md:340 references `--axis ds_layout_hasher` and USAGE.md:349-363 reference `ds_*` optimization axes, all hash-trie-specific.
- **Suggested fix:** Add `hash-trie` (hash-based) to the list of accepted structures, noting it pairs only with `-a hash-triejoin` (sorted tries pair with `-a leapfrog-triejoin`), and that `kermit join`'s LFTJ path supports only the two sorted tries. This reconciles the section with the hash-trie axes referenced later in the doc.
- **Verifier note:** CONFIRMED. USAGE.md:63-65 states `--indexstructure` "accepts `tree-trie` (pointer-based) or `column-trie` (column-oriented). Both implement the same `Relation` + `TrieIterable` traits and are interchangeable from the CLI's perspective." This is wrong on two counts. (1) A third index structure exists and is CLI-selectable: kermit-ds/src/ds/mod.rs:12-19 defines `IndexStructure { ColumnTrie, HashTrie, TreeTrie }`, and kermit/src/main.rs:101-106 exposes `HashTrie` via `IndexStructureSelector` for `bench ds`/`bench run`; main.rs:128-143 confirms hash-trie is a valid structure (paired with hash-triejoin). (2) The "interchangeable / same traits" framing is itself false because HashTrie lives in a different trait family (`HashTrieIterable`, not `TrieIterable`) and pairs only with `-a hash-triejoin` (main.rs:134, db.rs:300-307). Genuine internal inconsistency, as the finder claims: the same doc later documents `ablation --axis ds_layout_hasher` (USAGE.md:340) and the `ds_*` axes (USAGE.md:349-361), and those axes are hash-trie-specific (main.rs:170-209: `--ds-layout-hasher` is rejected on non-HashTrie selectors) — a reader cannot reconcile "only two structures exist" with a documented hash-trie-only ablation axis. The finder's caveat is accurate and verified: the bare `kermit join` path does genuinely exclude hash-trie — db.rs:286-303 (`instantiate_database`, called at main.rs:550) explicitly panics on `(HashTrie, HashTriejoin)` — so omitting hash-trie from join-only mechanics is defensible, but section 63-65 is headed "Pick the index structure" and makes a system-wide trait/completeness claim, so the omission and the false "interchangeable" assertion would mislead. Severity medium is correct: it omits a first-class shipping structure and states a flatly false interchangeability claim that the doc later contradicts, but it is usage prose rather than a crash/data-loss instruction, and the contradicting axis info is present elsewhere in the same file. Suggested fix (add hash-trie, note it pairs only with hash-triejoin and that `kermit join`'s LFTJ path supports only the two sorted tries) is appropriate.

### benchmarks/README.md — factual-drift
*unit: bench-readmes · location: ## Datalog query syntax, line 75 · finder severity: medium → verified: medium*

- **Claim:** Atoms are lower-case or numeric literals (match the `Term::Atom` variant).
- **Reality:** The parser only accepts atoms that are identifiers starting with an ASCII alphabetic character; there is no numeric-literal path. `ident` requires the first char to satisfy `c.is_ascii_alphabetic()` and the `term` parser classifies a lowercase-leading ident as `Term::Atom` and an uppercase-leading ident as `Term::Var` — a numeric token like `5` matches neither and fails to parse. The parser's own module/enum docs say atoms start with a lowercase letter (no mention of numerics).
- **Evidence:** kermit-parser/src/lib.rs:65 (`take_while(1.., |c: char| c.is_ascii_alphabetic())`), kermit-parser/src/lib.rs:89-101 (term -> Var/Atom only), kermit-parser/src/lib.rs:10 (`**Atoms** (constants) start with a lowercase letter`), kermit-parser/src/join_query.rs:3-4
- **Suggested fix:** Drop "or numeric literals" — state: "Atoms are identifiers starting with a lower-case letter (e.g. `alice`, `edge`); they map to the `Term::Atom` variant. Numeric/constant literals are not accepted by the parser (the WatDiv `c<id>` constants are an atom-name convention, not numeric tokens)."
- **Verifier note:** The doc at benchmarks/README.md:75 says "Atoms are lower-case or numeric literals (match the `Term::Atom` variant)." This misrepresents the parser. In kermit-parser/src/lib.rs:65, `ident` requires the first character to satisfy `c.is_ascii_alphabetic()` (`take_while(1.., |c: char| c.is_ascii_alphabetic())`), so a numeric literal cannot begin a token. In `term` (lib.rs:77-102) there are only three outcomes: `_` -> Placeholder, then `ident` classified as `Term::Var` if uppercase-leading (lib.rs:91-95) else `Term::Atom` (lib.rs:100) — there is no numeric-literal branch, so a bare numeric token like `5` matches neither and fails. This is confirmed by the explicit test `rejects_numeric_start_identifier` at lib.rs:429-431 asserting `123abc(X) :- Q(X).` fails. The parser's own docs contradict the README too: module doc lib.rs:10 ("**Atoms** (constants) start with a lowercase letter") and the `Term` enum doc at join_query.rs:3-4 ("atoms start with a lowercase letter"). Note: atoms MAY contain digits after the first alpha char (e.g. `edge2`, per the `identifiers_with_digits` test at lib.rs:370), but the doc's phrase "numeric literals" means number tokens, which are rejected. The finder's evidence and suggested fix ("Drop 'or numeric literals'; atoms are identifiers starting with a lower-case letter; numeric/constant literals are not accepted") are accurate. A reader would be misled into writing `R(5, X)` expecting it to parse. Medium is defensible (concrete user-facing syntax claim flatly contradicted by code and a dedicated test), though it sits at the low end since it is one clause in a niche subsection about constants.

### benchmarks/README.md — incompleteness
*unit: bench-readmes · location: ### Generator block (declarative), line 42 (and the surrounding generator section, lines 36-122) · finder severity: medium → verified: medium*

- **Claim:** | `generator.kind` | `watdiv` \| `lubm` | yes | Selects the underlying `kermit-rdf` pipeline. |  (the table and the whole generator section list only two kinds, watdiv and lubm)
- **Reality:** `GeneratorSpec` has THREE variants: `Watdiv`, `WatdivBasic` (YAML tag `kind: watdiv-basic`), and `Lubm`. The `watdiv-basic` kind drives `kermit_rdf::pipeline::run_basic_pipeline` (the 20 canonical L/S/F/C templates) and is exercised by a committed benchmark file. Neither README mentions `watdiv-basic` at all.
- **Evidence:** kermit-bench/src/definition.rs:43-59 (`enum GeneratorSpec { Watdiv, WatdivBasic { scale }, Lubm ... }` with `#[serde(rename_all = "kebab-case")]`), kermit-bench/src/definition.rs:306-315 (`validate_generator` handles `WatdivBasic`), kermit/src/materialize.rs:161-163 + 221-253 (`run_watdiv_basic`), benchmarks/watdiv-basic.yml:3-5 (`kind: watdiv-basic`). Verified neither README mentions it: grep -i 'watdiv-basic|basic' over both files returns no matches.
- **Suggested fix:** Add `watdiv-basic` to the `generator.kind` value list and add a short subsection/example documenting it (fixed 20-template L/S/F/C workload, only key is `scale`, no `stress` block), e.g. mirroring benchmarks/watdiv-basic.yml.
- **Verifier note:** CONFIRMED. benchmarks/README.md:42 lists generator.kind values as only `watdiv | lubm`, and the WatDiv subsection (README.md:45, "when `kind: watdiv`") plus the whole generator section (lines 36-122) never mention `watdiv-basic`; grep -i for "watdiv-basic"/"basic" over benchmarks/README.md and kermit-rdf/src/lubm/README.md returns zero hits. The code has a THIRD variant: kermit-bench/src/definition.rs:43-59 defines `enum GeneratorSpec` with `Watdiv`, `WatdivBasic { scale }`, and `Lubm` under `#[serde(tag="kind", rename_all="kebab-case")]`, so `WatdivBasic` deserializes from `kind: watdiv-basic`. It is validated (definition.rs:306-315), wired in kermit/src/materialize.rs:161-163 -> run_watdiv_basic (221-253) -> kermit_rdf::pipeline::run_basic_pipeline (a real public fn at kermit-rdf/src/pipeline.rs:261, distinct from run_pipeline, driving the 20 canonical L/S/F/C templates), tested (definition.rs:880-917), and shipped as a committed benchmark at benchmarks/watdiv-basic.yml:3-5 (`kind: watdiv-basic`, `scale: 10`). The README's schema is genuinely incomplete: a reader cannot author or understand watdiv-basic from it. Severity medium is correct: a full, committed, tested generator kind (1 of 3) is undocumented (real incompleteness with a user-facing example file), but nothing in the doc is actively false/misleading and the feature is discoverable via the committed YAML and source, so it does not rise to high/critical. The finder's suggested fix (add watdiv-basic to the kind value list plus a short subsection: fixed 20-template L/S/F/C workload, only key is `scale`, no `stress` block, mirroring watdiv-basic.yml) is accurate.

### docs/benchmarks/README.md — internal-inconsistency
*unit: bench-readmes · location: ## Cross-benchmark comparison table, line 26 (row "Query count per dataset"); cf. Index, line 14 · finder severity: medium → verified: medium*

- **Claim:** | Query count per dataset | 14 | 12 400 across 12 stress files |
- **Reality:** The ~12,400 figure is PER stress workload/file, not a total spanning all 12 files. Line 14 of the same doc says "~12 400 mechanically-generated stress queries per workload", and WATDIV.md states ~12,400 per file; the committed `watdiv-stress-100-test-1.yml` contains exactly 12,400 queries. "12 400 across 12 stress files" reads as a grand total, which would be ~148,800, contradicting line 14 and the data.
- **Evidence:** docs/benchmarks/README.md:14 ("~12 400 ... per workload") vs docs/benchmarks/README.md:26 ("12 400 across 12 stress files"); docs/benchmarks/WATDIV.md:6 ("each stress workload contains ~12 400 ... queries") and WATDIV.md:70-73 ("~12 400 each"); `grep -c 'query:' benchmarks/watdiv-stress-100-test-1.yml` = 12400.
- **Suggested fix:** Change the WatDiv cell to make the per-file count explicit, e.g. "~12 400 per stress file (12 committed files)" so it agrees with line 14 and WATDIV.md.
- **Verifier note:** Confirmed internal inconsistency. docs/benchmarks/README.md:26 reads `| Query count per dataset | 14 | 12 400 across 12 stress files |`. The 12,400 figure is PER stress file/workload, not a span/total across 12 files: I verified all 12 committed stress YAMLs (watdiv-stress-{100,1000}-{test-1..5,warmup}.yml) each contain exactly 12,400 `query:` lines via grep; the true total across the 12 files is 148,800 (12 × 12,400). The phrasing "12 400 across 12 stress files" contradicts three other places: (a) README.md:14 "~12 400 mechanically-generated stress queries per workload"; (b) the row's own header "Query count per dataset" (the LUBM cell "14" is the per-dataset count, per LUBM.md:5,8), so the row is per-dataset on both sides; (c) WATDIV.md:6-7 "each stress workload contains ~12 400" and WATDIV.md:70-73 "~12 400 each". "X across N files" naturally reads as a total/distribution, inviting a ~12× misread (a reader would infer either ~148,800 total or ~1,033/file). This is a citation-grade comparison table (README.md:107 treats it as the canonical matrix), so the ambiguity matters; medium is appropriate (correct number is present and corroborated nearby, so it's a wording inconsistency rather than a flat-wrong figure or broken code claim). Finder's suggested fix ("~12 400 per stress file (12 committed files)") resolves it.

### docs/benchmarks/WATDIV.md — factual-drift
*unit: bench-watdiv · location: Workload reference table (lines 203-208) and lines 113-115 · finder severity: high → verified: medium*

- **Claim:** Workload-reference table lists `constants-per-query | Number of c<id> constants pinning each query` and `allow-join-vertex | Whether stress templates can include join vertices`; and line 113-114 says 'The four stress flags (--max-query-size, --query-count, --constants-per-query, --allow-join-vertex) shape the workload itself'.
- **Reality:** Only --max-query-size and --query-count are forwarded to the vendored binary's -s invocation. --constants-per-query and --allow-join-vertex are explicitly NOT passed to the binary; they are recorded in meta.json for provenance only. StressParams docstrings say verbatim: '**NOT forwarded** to the current vendored binary — recorded in meta.json for provenance only. Will be wired up when the binary gains a matching flag.' The driver's run_stress call passes only max_query_size and query_count.
- **Evidence:** kermit-rdf/src/driver/mod.rs:23-27 (constants_per_query / allow_join_vertex doc-comments: 'NOT forwarded'); kermit-rdf/src/driver/mod.rs:94-100 (run_stress called with only stress.max_query_size and stress.query_count); kermit-rdf/src/driver/invoke.rs:147-158 (run_stress signature/args: -s <model> <data_nt> <max_query_size> <count> only)
- **Suggested fix:** Mark --constants-per-query and --allow-join-vertex in the table as 'recorded in meta.json for provenance only; NOT currently forwarded to the vendored binary (no effect on generated data/queries)'. Change line 113-114 to say only two of the four flags currently shape the workload.
- **Verifier note:** Confirmed factual drift. WATDIV.md line 113-114 claims "The four stress flags (--max-query-size, --query-count, --constants-per-query, --allow-join-vertex) shape the workload itself," and the Workload-reference table (docs/benchmarks/WATDIV.md:203-208) describes constants-per-query as "Number of c<id> constants pinning each query" and allow-join-vertex as "Whether stress templates can include join vertices," with lines 210-213 reinforcing all four "are settable on the command line" — no caveat. The code contradicts this: kermit-rdf/src/driver/mod.rs:20-27 docstrings state verbatim that constants_per_query and allow_join_vertex are "**NOT forwarded** to the current vendored binary — recorded in meta.json for provenance only." The driver (mod.rs:94-100) calls run_stress with only stress.max_query_size and stress.query_count; run_stress (invoke.rs:147-158) builds the -s command with only model_file, data_nt, max_q_str, count_str; and run_queries (invoke.rs:194-207) passes only count_per_template plus a hardcoded recurrence="1". The two params do flow into StressParamsMeta → meta.json (kermit-rdf/src/pipeline.rs:88-110), confirming the "provenance only" half. So a reader setting --constants-per-query/--allow-join-vertex would get identical generated data/queries — the doc overstates two of the four knobs. Real and misleading. Downgraded high→medium: only 2 of 4 flags affected, they are inert rather than silently producing wrong benchmark data (the other two work as documented), so no data-integrity hazard — just documentation overstating effect. Finder's evidence and suggested fix are accurate.

### docs/data-structures/hash-trie.md — internal-inconsistency
*unit: doc-inventory · location: ## Invariants line 38 vs ## Optimizations 'Layout options' lines 104-114 · finder severity: medium → verified: medium*

- **Claim:** The Invariants section (line 38) describes the hash mechanism as a function `hash_attribute(depth, key)` with a vestigial depth parameter, while the Optimizations / Layout options section (lines 104-114) describes the SAME mechanism as `HashTrie<H: HashStrategy>` with `SipHashStrategy`/`FxHashStrategy` selected via `-i hash-trie --ds-layout-hasher`.
- **Reality:** Only the Optimizations-section description matches the code (`HashStrategy` trait, `H::hash(key)`). The two sections of the same doc describe the hashing API two mutually incompatible ways; the Invariants version refers to an API that does not exist.
- **Evidence:** docs/data-structures/hash-trie.md:38 (hash_attribute narrative) vs docs/data-structures/hash-trie.md:104-114 (HashStrategy/SipHashStrategy/FxHashStrategy); code confirms only the latter: kermit-iters/src/hash_strategy.rs:13-16 and kermit-ds/src/ds/hash_trie/implementation.rs:42 (`pub struct HashTrie<H: HashStrategy = SipHashStrategy>`).
- **Suggested fix:** Make the Invariants 'Hash function consistency' bullet consistent with the Optimizations section: reference `HashStrategy::hash` / `H::hash` and the `ds_layout_hasher` axis rather than the obsolete `hash_attribute(depth, key)`.
- **Verifier note:** Confirmed internal inconsistency in docs/data-structures/hash-trie.md. The Invariants "Hash function consistency" bullet at line 38 describes the hashing mechanism as a function `hash_attribute(depth, key)` "exported from kermit-iters", imported by both `HashTrie::insert_at` and `SingletonHashTrieIter::new`, with a vestigial depth param kept for ABI compatibility. The Optimizations/Layout-options section at lines 104-114 describes the SAME mechanism completely differently: `HashTrie<H: HashStrategy>` with `SipHashStrategy`/`FxHashStrategy` selected via `-i hash-trie --ds-layout-hasher`. Code matches ONLY the Optimizations description: kermit-iters/src/hash_strategy.rs:13-16 defines `trait HashStrategy { fn hash(key: usize) -> u64 }` (no depth param); kermit-ds/src/ds/hash_trie/implementation.rs:42 is `pub struct HashTrie<H: HashStrategy = SipHashStrategy>` and line 97 calls `let hash = H::hash(key);` (not hash_attribute). A repo-wide grep for `hash_attribute` returns ZERO .rs source hits — it survives only in older design/plan docs and this doc. In fact docs/specs/2026-05-27-optimization-standard-design.md:204 ("hash_attribute removed") and docs/plans/2026-05-27-optimization-standard-plan.md:50 ("Remove legacy hash_attribute function") document its deletion. The finder slightly understated the error: kermit-algos/src/hash_singleton.rs:5-10,51 shows SingletonHashTrieIter takes a PRECOMPUTED hash and "does not depend on any strategy" — so line 38's claim that the singleton imports the hashing function is wrong both in name and in architecture. This is a self-contradicting invariant in a load-bearing component doc (Priorities item 3) that would send a contributor hunting for a non-existent `kermit-iters::hash_attribute`. Medium is correct: misleading but not a code-correctness/safety bug, and the same doc carries the right info elsewhere. The suggested fix (rewrite the bullet around HashStrategy::hash / ds_layout_hasher) is appropriate.

### docs/data-structures/hash-trie.md — factual-drift
*unit: ds-hash · location: Invariants section, "Hash function consistency" bullet (line 38) · finder severity: high → verified: medium*

- **Claim:** `hash_attribute(depth, key)` is the single source of truth for the hashing convention, exported from `kermit-iters`. It ignores the `depth` parameter and hashes only the key value. The depth parameter is preserved in the signature for ABI compatibility with the original design but does not affect the output. ... Both `HashTrie::insert_at` and `SingletonHashTrieIter::new` import this function; any divergence silently breaks queries against constants.
- **Reality:** No function named `hash_attribute` exists anywhere in the repository (grep for `hash_attribute` over all `*.rs` returns zero hits). Hashing is done through the `HashStrategy` trait's one-argument method `fn hash(key: usize) -> u64` (no `depth` parameter at all). `HashTrie::insert_at` calls `H::hash(key)`; `SingletonHashTrieIter::new(value, hash)` takes a precomputed hash and does NOT import or call any hashing function (its module doc explicitly states it does not depend on any strategy). The two-argument depth-ignoring `hash_attribute` and its claimed ABI-compat depth parameter are entirely fictional / describe a removed pre-refactor design.
- **Evidence:** kermit-iters/src/hash_strategy.rs:13-16 (trait `HashStrategy` with `fn hash(key: usize) -> u64`); kermit-ds/src/ds/hash_trie/implementation.rs:96-97 (`let key = tuple[depth]; let hash = H::hash(key);`); kermit-algos/src/hash_singleton.rs:9-10 ("this file does not depend on any strategy") and :51 (`pub fn new(value: usize, hash: u64) -> Self`); `grep -rn hash_attribute --include=*.rs .` returns no matches.
- **Suggested fix:** Rewrite the bullet to describe the actual mechanism: the hash is produced by the compile-time `HashStrategy` parameter `H` via `H::hash(key)` (single `usize` argument, no depth), defined in `kermit_iters::hash_strategy` (`SipHashStrategy`/`FxHashStrategy`). Note that `HashTrie::insert_at` calls `H::hash`, while `SingletonHashTrieIter` is handed a precomputed hash by `kermit::db::hash_join` (which uses the same `H`). Remove the `hash_attribute(depth, key)` name, the depth parameter, the ABI-compatibility rationale, and the claim that `SingletonHashTrieIter::new` imports the function.
- **Verifier note:** The doc bullet (docs/data-structures/hash-trie.md:38) is confirmed to misrepresent the code. It claims a function `hash_attribute(depth, key)` exported from `kermit-iters`, with a depth parameter kept "for ABI compatibility", imported by both `HashTrie::insert_at` and `SingletonHashTrieIter::new`. Every concrete claim is false: (1) `grep -rn hash_attribute --include='*.rs'` returns zero hits — no such function exists. (2) Hashing is `HashStrategy::hash(key: usize) -> u64`, a single-argument trait method with no depth parameter (kermit-iters/src/hash_strategy.rs:13-16); the depth param and ABI rationale are fabricated. (3) kermit-iters/src/lib.rs:45-53 exports `HashStrategy`/`SipHashStrategy`/`FxHashStrategy`, not `hash_attribute`. (4) `HashTrie::insert_at` calls `H::hash(key)` (kermit-ds/src/ds/hash_trie/implementation.rs:96-97). (5) `SingletonHashTrieIter::new(value, hash)` takes a precomputed hash and imports no hashing function; its module doc explicitly states "this file does not depend on any strategy" (kermit-algos/src/hash_singleton.rs:9-10,51). The hash is computed by the caller `kermit::db::hash_join` via `H::hash(id)` (kermit/src/db.rs:261-262), exactly as the suggested fix describes. This is genuine factual drift naming a nonexistent API plus a false import relationship. Severity adjusted high→medium: the bullet's core invariant (per-column physical tables prevent cross-attribute collision; divergence breaks constant queries) is still sound, and the same file's Layout-options section (lines 104-114) already documents the real `HashStrategy` mechanism correctly, so the doc is locally self-contradicting rather than wholesale wrong. The finder's reality assertion and evidence are accurate; the suggested rewrite is correct.

### docs/data-structures/hash-trie.md — internal-inconsistency
*unit: ds-hash · location: Invariants "Hash function consistency" (line 38) vs. Optimizations → Layout options (lines 104-114) · finder severity: medium → verified: medium*
