Note: the inline parenthetical count in the intro paragraph also says "five" and would need updating too for a complete fix.

### python/kermit-lab/notebooks/00_full_timeline.ipynb — factual-drift
*unit: notebooks · location: Phase 4 markdown cell (id 8a1fd95c) · finder severity: medium → verified: medium*

- **Claim:** 'kl.load(glob, criterion_root=…) parses every BenchReport matching the glob, joins it against the Criterion JSON artefacts … and returns a tidy long-form DataFrame: one row per (query, data_structure, algorithm, phase, sample_index).'
- **Reality:** kl.load returns the SUMMARY frame — one row per (report × criterion_group), with no per-sample granularity and no sample_index column. The per-iteration / per-sample frame (which has sample_idx and per_iter_ns) is produced by the separate kl.load_samples, not kl.load.
- **Evidence:** frame.py:162-173 load() docstring: 'One row per (report × criterion_group).'; frame.py:121-124 builds rows via iter_function_data which yields one (report, gref, data) per function (loader.py:111-124). The sample-level columns sample_idx/per_iter_ns appear only in _samples_from_reports (frame.py:188,191), which backs load_samples (frame.py:199-210). The summary frame has no sample column.
- **Suggested fix:** Reword to describe load()'s actual granularity, e.g. 'one row per (configuration, query, metric, phase/relation)' (matching the wording already used correctly in 07's Phase 3 cell), and note that per-sample rows come from kl.load_samples.
- **Verifier note:** CONFIRMED. The doc (python/kermit-lab/notebooks/00_full_timeline.ipynb, Phase 4 markdown cell id 8a1fd95c) states verbatim that kl.load 'returns a tidy long-form DataFrame: one row per (query, data_structure, algorithm, phase, sample_index)'. The code contradicts this: frame.py:156-173 load() returns the SUMMARY frame, with its own docstring (frame.py:165-166) stating 'One row per (report × criterion_group)'. Rows are built (frame.py:121-124) from iter_function_data, which (loader.py:111-124) yields exactly one (report, group_ref, FunctionData) per Criterion function — function/summary granularity, never per-sample. The per-sample columns (sample_idx, per_iter_ns) exist only in _samples_from_reports (frame.py:188,191), backing the SEPARATE load_samples (frame.py:199-210, docstring: 'One row per Criterion sample point'). The exact column name the doc cites — 'sample_index' — appears nowhere in the package (grep over kermit_lab/ returns nothing; the real column is 'sample_idx'). Corroboration: the sibling notebook 07_lubm_reference_comparison.ipynb Phase 3 cell describes kl.load correctly as 'one row per (configuration, query, metric, phase / relation)', matching both the code and the finder's suggested fix — so the 00 wording is genuine drift, not an intentional alternate convention. Impact justifies medium: a reader following this onboarding cell would index df['sample_index'] (KeyError) or assume load already carries per-iteration data and never call load_samples; not high because it is a notebook cell with the correct docstring one click away, not low because it invents a phantom column that causes outright failures.

### python/kermit-lab/notebooks/00_full_timeline.ipynb — factual-drift
*unit: notebooks · location: Phase 2 markdown cell (id f1d6805d) · finder severity: medium → verified: medium*

- **Claim:** 'bench run reads the cached benchmark, builds both index structures (TreeTrie, ColumnTrie) for each LUBM query, and times the LeapfrogTriejoin algorithm … -i all expands to the full IndexStructure enum; -a leapfrog-triejoin pins the algorithm.' (The Phase 2 cell e49851d2 invokes `bench … run lubm-1-nb-fulltimeline -i all -a leapfrog-triejoin --metrics iteration`.)
- **Reality:** -i all expands to all THREE structures (ColumnTrie, HashTrie, TreeTrie). The bench run cross-product loop does NOT filter incompatible (structure, algorithm) pairs inside the loop — it only gates the *selectors* up front, and `All` is permissive. So with -i all -a leapfrog-triejoin the loop reaches the HashTrie arm with algo=LeapfrogTriejoin and calls run_benchmark_hash, which unconditionally runs hash_join (the algorithm argument is ignored) while stamping the report's algorithm axis as 'LeapfrogTriejoin'. Result: three reports are produced, including a spurious/mislabeled HashTrie one — not 'both (TreeTrie, ColumnTrie)'. This is the known `bench run -i all` cross-product bug; the in-code comment claiming the gate 'ensures we only reach this arm with JoinAlgorithm::HashTriejoin' is itself false for -a leapfrog-triejoin.
- **Evidence:** IndexStructureSelector::All expands via IndexStructure::value_variants() (kermit/src/main.rs:112); the enum has ColumnTrie/HashTrie/TreeTrie (main.rs:103-105). The bench run loop iterates all indexstructures × all algorithms with no per-pair compatibility check (main.rs:1547-1608); the HashTrie arm dispatches to run_benchmark_hash regardless of algo (main.rs:1581-1604) and the misleading comment is at main.rs:1573-1575. run_benchmark_hash calls hash_join unconditionally (main.rs:1188) and hash_join takes no algorithm parameter (kermit/src/db.rs:230), yet the report's algorithm axis is set from the passed-through algo (main.rs:1117,1219). Note 07's Phase 2 cell (id f81e12df) documents this exact bug and works around it by enumerating the three valid configs.
- **Suggested fix:** Either pin the structure explicitly (e.g. run TreeTrie and ColumnTrie in separate invocations, as notebook 07 does) so HashTrie is never reached, or acknowledge that -i all currently also emits a (mislabeled) HashTrie report under -a leapfrog-triejoin and adjust the 'builds both index structures' wording accordingly.
- **Verifier note:** The doc claim is genuinely wrong and would mislead a reader. Notebook 00 cell f1d6805d says `bench run ... -i all -a leapfrog-triejoin` "builds both index structures (TreeTrie, ColumnTrie)" and "-a leapfrog-triejoin pins the algorithm." Verified against code:  (1) -i all (IndexStructureSelector::All) expands via IndexStructure::value_variants() (kermit/src/main.rs:112) to THREE variants — ColumnTrie, HashTrie, TreeTrie (kermit-ds/src/ds/mod.rs:12-19) — not "both".  (2) The upfront gate at main.rs:1529 passes for (All, LeapfrogTriejoin) because supports_algorithm returns true for any (All, _) pair (main.rs:132). -a leapfrog-triejoin expands to exactly [LeapfrogTriejoin] (main.rs:165).  (3) The cross-product loop (main.rs:1547-1608) dispatches solely on `ds` (match ds) with NO per-pair compatibility check or continue. The HashTrie arm (main.rs:1581-1604) is therefore reached with algo=LeapfrogTriejoin and calls run_benchmark_hash.  (4) run_benchmark_hash calls hash_join unconditionally (main.rs:1188); hash_join (kermit/src/db.rs:230) takes NO algorithm parameter — the `algorithm` arg only feeds algo_name (main.rs:1117), which is stamped into the report metadata, group name, and the "algorithm" axis (main.rs:1151, 1219). So a third report is produced that actually runs the hash join but is labeled algorithm=LeapfrogTriejoin.  The finder's asserted reality is accurate in every load-bearing detail, including the subtle point that this specific flag combo does NOT panic: the panic in the known -i all -a all bug (project memory) occurs on the sorted-trie path via instantiate_database at db.rs:312 for (TreeTrie/ColumnTrie, HashTriejoin); the HashTrie+LeapfrogTriejoin case never touches instantiate_database, so it silently mislabels instead. The in-code comment at main.rs:1573-1575 ("the supports_algorithm gate above ensures we only reach this arm with JoinAlgorithm::HashTriejoin") is itself false for -a leapfrog-triejoin.  The same repo contradicts notebook 00: notebook 07 cell f81e12df states "-i all -a all cross-product does not skip the invalid structure/algorithm pairings, so we enumerate the three valid ones explicitly," and its intro (d59ad576) notes (HashTrie, HashTriejoin) is the only valid HashTrie pairing.  Severity medium is correct: not critical/high (the notebook's Phase 5 plots filter to TreeTrie for bar_queries and group by data_structure elsewhere, so the spurious HashTrie row surfaces as an unexpected extra series rather than crashing or corrupting the TreeTrie/ColumnTrie data), but more than low because this is the designated entry-point teaching notebook (cell 83944ad2) and the claim teaches wrong -i all/-a semantics while injecting a mislabeled hash-join row (labeled LeapfrogTriejoin) into the DataFrame. Fix: pin tree-trie/column-trie in separate invocations as notebook 07 does, or reword to acknowledge -i all also emits a (mislabeled) HashTrie report.

## Low-severity findings (57)


### ARCHITECTURE.md — factual-drift
*unit: architecture · location: Workspace Structure → Dependency Graph, prose (line 44) · finder severity: low → verified: low*

- **Claim:** "The `kermit` binary depends on every other crate."
- **Reality:** The `kermit` binary directly depends on 6 of the 7 other workspace crates. `kermit-derive` is not a direct dependency of `kermit` (it is pulled in only transitively via `kermit-ds`).
- **Evidence:** kermit/Cargo.toml `[dependencies]` lists kermit-ds, kermit-iters, kermit-algos, kermit-bench, kermit-parser, kermit-rdf — but not kermit-derive. There are 7 sibling crates total (kermit-iters, kermit-derive, kermit-parser, kermit-ds, kermit-algos, kermit-bench, kermit-rdf).
- **Suggested fix:** Soften to e.g. 'The `kermit` binary depends (directly or transitively) on every other crate' or 'directly depends on all sibling crates except the proc-macro crate `kermit-derive`, which it pulls in transitively.'
- **Verifier note:** Doc claim verified at ARCHITECTURE.md:44 ("The `kermit` binary depends on every other crate."). The asserted reality is accurate: the workspace has 8 members (Cargo.toml members list) = kermit + 7 siblings (kermit-iters, kermit-derive, kermit-parser, kermit-ds, kermit-algos, kermit-bench, kermit-rdf). kermit/Cargo.toml `[dependencies]` (lines 13-18) directly lists only 6 siblings — kermit-ds, kermit-iters, kermit-algos, kermit-bench, kermit-parser, kermit-rdf — and NOT kermit-derive. kermit-derive is a direct dependency only of kermit-ds (kermit-ds/Cargo.toml:17) and kermit-algos, so kermit reaches it only transitively. A `grep -rln kermit-derive */Cargo.toml` confirms kermit is absent from the direct dependents.  This is a genuine, reader-misleading drift rather than a stylistic nit because the sentence sits among neighboring claims that are unambiguously about DIRECT edges (e.g. "`kermit-rdf` depends on `kermit-parser`, `kermit-ds`, and `kermit-bench`" matches kermit-rdf's direct deps exactly), and the adjacent dependency-graph diagram (lines 28-42) draws only direct edges and shows no kermit→kermit-derive arrow (kermit-derive's sole arrow is `kermit-iters ◄─── kermit-derive` at line 29). Under that consistent direct-dependency reading the claim is false; it is true only under a transitive reading. Severity low is correct: it is a single-crate omission of a proc-macro crate that does not distort the core architecture, and the finder's suggested fix (clarify "directly or transitively", or "all siblings except the proc-macro crate kermit-derive, pulled in transitively") is accurate — kermit→kermit-ds→kermit-derive confirms transitive reachability.

### BENCHMARKING.md — factual-drift
*unit: benchmarking · location: Section "What you can measure", line 38 · finder severity: medium → verified: low*

- **Claim:** "Every `bench` subcommand accepts `--metrics`, defaulting to all three. They fall into two families."
- **Reality:** `--metrics` is NOT a shared/parent flag and is NOT accepted by `bench join`. It is declared only on the `Ds` and `Run` subcommand variants. The shared `BenchArgs` struct (name, sample-size, measurement-time, warm-up-time, report-json) has no `metrics` field, and the `Join` variant flattens only `QueryArgs` + an `output` path. `bench join` performs a single time-only measurement of the whole `db.join` call (build_time_criterion, one bench_function, ReportMetric::Time) with no insertion/iteration/space split and no way to select metrics. So neither the existence claim nor the "two families" framing applies to `bench join`.
- **Evidence:** kermit/src/main.rs:251-273 (BenchArgs has no metrics field); kermit/src/main.rs:283-290 (Join variant: only query_args + output, no metrics); kermit/src/main.rs:308-316 (Ds variant declares `metrics: Vec<Metric>`); kermit/src/main.rs:350-358 (Run variant declares `metrics`); kermit/src/main.rs:1420-1450 (bench join dispatch: single time-only Criterion bench_function, ReportMetric::Time, no metric selection); kermit/src/main.rs:498-503 (BenchArgs flattened separately from BenchSubcommand)
- **Suggested fix:** Qualify the claim, e.g.: "`bench ds` and `bench run` accept `--metrics`, defaulting to all three (`insertion`, `iteration`, `space`). `bench join` is time-only and has no `--metrics` flag — it times the full query each iteration."
- **Verifier note:** Doc BENCHMARKING.md:38-39 says "Every `bench` subcommand accepts `--metrics`, defaulting to all three." This is false. In kermit/src/main.rs the shared `BenchArgs` struct (lines 250-273: name, sample_size, measurement_time, warm_up_time, report_json) has no `metrics` field, and it is the only thing flattened at the parent `Bench` command level (lines 498-504). `metrics: Vec<Metric>` is declared only on the `Ds` variant (308-316) and `Run` variant (350-358). The `Join` variant flattens only `QueryArgs` + `output` (283-290), and its dispatch arm destructures `{ query_args, output }` only (1392-1395), performing a single time-only Criterion measurement of `db.join` (build_time_criterion + one bench_function via iter_batched, emitting one CriterionGroupRef with ReportMetric::Time, lines 1420-1456) — no insertion/iteration/space split, no metric selection. A grep confirms `metrics` is consumed only in the Ds/Run code paths (645-1203, dispatch 1462/1520), never in the Join path. So `kermit bench join ... --metrics ...` would be a clap parse error, and the "two families" framing does not apply to it (nor to list/fetch/clean/gen). The finder's reality and suggested qualification are accurate. Downgraded to low because the inaccuracy is one over-broad universal quantifier; the practical core (ds/run accept --metrics with that default, and the insertion/iteration/space table) is correct, so the misleading surface is limited to the bench-join edge.

### CLAUDE.md — factual-drift
*unit: claude-md · location: Section 'Extending the System' > 'Adding a new join algorithm', step 4 (line 132); also referenced in step 6 of 'Adding a new index structure' wording is fine, but the algorithm recipe says: 'Add a variant to `JoinAlgorithmSelector` in `kermit/src/main.rs` (around line 124)' · finder severity: low → verified: low*

- **Reality:** The `JoinAlgorithmSelector` enum is declared at kermit/src/main.rs:153 (its `impl ... expand()` at line 159-167). Line 124 falls inside the doc-comment of the unrelated `supports_algorithm` method on `IndexStructureSelector`. The companion claim for `IndexStructureSelector` 'around line 99' is accurate (enum at line 101), so only the line-124 reference is meaningfully off (~29 lines).
- **Evidence:** kermit/src/main.rs:153 `enum JoinAlgorithmSelector {`; kermit/src/main.rs:159 `impl JoinAlgorithmSelector {`; kermit/src/main.rs:101 `enum IndexStructureSelector {` (confirms the 'around line 99' claim); line 124 is inside the `supports_algorithm` doc-comment.
- **Suggested fix:** Change 'around line 124' to 'around line 153' (or drop the brittle line number in favor of just naming the enum, as line numbers drift on every edit).
- **Verifier note:** Doc claim verified verbatim at CLAUDE.md:132: "Add a variant to `JoinAlgorithmSelector` in `kermit/src/main.rs` (around line 124) and to its `expand()` method." The asserted reality is accurate: `enum JoinAlgorithmSelector {` is at kermit/src/main.rs:153, and its `impl ... expand()` is at lines 159-167. Line 124 falls inside the doc-comment of the unrelated `supports_algorithm` method on `IndexStructureSelector` (that doc-comment spans lines 119-127; line 124 reads "either side is permissive — the cross-product caller in `bench"). The companion claim "around line 99" for `IndexStructureSelector` is also confirmed correct (enum at line 101, doc-comment from line 96), so only the line-124 reference is off — by ~29 lines, and it points into the wrong symbol entirely. This is a genuine factual-drift discrepancy. Severity is correctly "low": the enum is named explicitly and unambiguously in the same sentence (`JoinAlgorithmSelector`), so a reader would grep the symbol and land on line 153 immediately; the parenthetical line number is a convenience hint, and the companion guidance "and to its `expand()` method" is accurate. Suggested fix is sound — either bump to "around line 153" or drop the brittle line number and rely on the symbol name.

### CLAUDE.md — stale-content
*unit: claude-md · location: Section 'Priorities', item 1 (line 13); also referenced in 'Adding an optimization' recipe step 5 (line 154) · finder severity: medium → verified: low*

- **Claim:** Config and BuildMode dimensions are tested via `define_multiway_join_test_suite_with_config!` and `define_multiway_join_test_suite_for_build_mode!` respectively.
- **Reality:** Neither macro exists anywhere in the codebase. The only macros defined in kermit/tests/common/macros.rs are define_multiway_join_test!, the 11 per-pattern macros, and define_multiway_join_test_suite!. A repo-wide grep for the two names finds zero definitions or usages in .rs files. The doc's own linked spec, docs/specs/optimization-standard.md, explicitly calls define_multiway_join_test_suite_with_config! 'prescribed (but not-yet-implemented)' (line 340) and says each macro 'lands with the first Config/BuildMode consumer' (lines 63, 79). CLAUDE.md presents them as current mechanisms without that caveat, contradicting the spec it links.
- **Evidence:** kermit/tests/common/macros.rs: only macros are at lines 2, 30, 51, 73, 95, 116, 137, 158, 179, 199, 222, 247, 268 (none named *_with_config or *_for_build_mode); repo-wide `grep -rn 'define_multiway_join_test_suite_with_config|define_multiway_join_test_suite_for_build_mode' --include=*.rs` => no matches; docs/specs/optimization-standard.md:340 'Use the prescribed (but not-yet-implemented) `define_multiway_join_test_suite_with_config!` macro variant. For now, write per-config tests by hand'; lines 63 and 79 '(macro lands with the first Config/BuildMode consumer)'.
- **Suggested fix:** Reword item 1 to mark these as not-yet-implemented, matching the spec, e.g. 'Config and BuildMode dimensions are to be tested via `define_multiway_join_test_suite_with_config!` / `define_multiway_join_test_suite_for_build_mode!` (not yet implemented — until the first consumer lands, write per-config/per-mode tests by hand; see docs/specs/optimization-standard.md).'
- **Verifier note:** CONFIRMED. CLAUDE.md:13 (Priorities item 1) states without caveat: "Config and BuildMode dimensions are tested via `define_multiway_join_test_suite_with_config!` and `define_multiway_join_test_suite_for_build_mode!` respectively" — present tense, implying these macros exist and are the current mechanism. Line 154 likewise says "new macro invocations for Config/BuildMode". Reality verified: the only macros in kermit/tests/common/macros.rs are define_multiway_join_test! (line 2), the 11 per-pattern macros (lines 30-247), and define_multiway_join_test_suite! (line 268). A repo-wide grep for both names returns zero hits in any .rs file — they appear ONLY in markdown. The linked spec docs/specs/optimization-standard.md contradicts CLAUDE.md: line 63 and line 79 each say the macro "lands with the first Config/BuildMode consumer", and line 340 explicitly calls define_multiway_join_test_suite_with_config! "(but not-yet-implemented)" and instructs "For now, write per-config tests by hand". The design doc (2026-05-27-optimization-standard-design.md:23) calls them "planned". So CLAUDE.md misrepresents not-yet-implemented, deferred macros as current testing infrastructure — a reader following Priorities item 1 to add a Config/BuildMode optimization would search for/try to invoke macros that do not exist. The finding is accurate and the suggested fix (mark as not-yet-implemented, defer to the spec) is correct. Severity lowered from medium to low: the Layout path and the core define_multiway_join_test_suite! claim are correct, no Config/BuildMode consumers currently exist (the macros are deferred precisely until the first one lands), so the misleading sentence only bites the forward-looking first-consumer case, and the accurate spec is one linked click away in the same sentence.

### CLAUDE.md — factual-drift
*unit: claude-md · location: Section 'Workspace Architecture' > 'Dependency flow' paragraph (line 91) · finder severity: low → verified: low*

- **Claim:** `kermit-iters` → `kermit-derive`, `kermit-parser` → `kermit-ds` → `kermit-algos` (also depends on `kermit-parser`) ...
- **Reality:** Read as a chain, the segment 'kermit-parser → kermit-ds' asserts kermit-ds depends on kermit-parser, but it does not: kermit-ds/Cargo.toml lists only kermit-iters and kermit-derive as internal deps, and no kermit-ds source references kermit_parser. kermit-parser is consumed by kermit-algos (correctly noted by the parenthetical) and kermit-rdf, not by kermit-ds. The line is ambiguous prose (could be read as a comma-separated list of foundational crates), hence low severity, but the literal arrow chain is incorrect.
- **Evidence:** kermit-ds/Cargo.toml [dependencies]: kermit-iters, kermit-derive only (no kermit-parser); `grep -rn 'kermit_parser|kermit-parser' kermit-ds/src/` => no matches. kermit-algos/Cargo.toml does list kermit-parser (confirming the parenthetical); kermit-rdf/Cargo.toml lists kermit-parser, kermit-ds, kermit-bench.
- **Suggested fix:** Disambiguate so the chain does not imply kermit-ds depends on kermit-parser, e.g. 'kermit-iters → kermit-derive; kermit-iters/kermit-derive → kermit-ds → kermit-algos (which also depends on kermit-parser); kermit-parser is otherwise a standalone leaf consumed by kermit-algos and kermit-rdf.'
- **Verifier note:** Confirmed. CLAUDE.md:91 reads "`kermit-iters` → `kermit-derive`, `kermit-parser` → `kermit-ds` → `kermit-algos` (also depends on `kermit-parser`)". The segment places `kermit-parser` on the left of `→ kermit-ds`, implying kermit-parser is an upstream input to kermit-ds. It is not: kermit-ds/Cargo.toml lists only kermit-iters and kermit-derive as internal deps (no kermit-parser), and `grep -rn 'kermit_parser|kermit-parser' kermit-ds/src/` returns no matches (exit 1). kermit-parser is in fact a leaf with no internal kermit deps, consumed only by kermit-algos (kermit-algos/Cargo.toml:15) and kermit-rdf (kermit-rdf/Cargo.toml:13), plus the kermit binary. Every other claim on the line checks out: kermit-derive depends only on kermit-iters (derive/Cargo.toml:21); the parenthetical "(also depends on kermit-parser)" for kermit-algos is correct; kermit-rdf "(depends on parser, ds, bench)" matches kermit-rdf/Cargo.toml:13-15. The finder's evidence is fully accurate. Severity stays low: the parenthetical partly self-corrects the parser relationship, the comma/arrow-mixed prose reads as informal grouping rather than a strict topological chain, and no build/test/CLI recipe is broken — but the literal arrow chain is genuinely wrong and could mislead a reader into thinking kermit-ds may use kermit-parser types.

### CLAUDE.md — factual-drift
*unit: doc-inventory · location: ## Gotchas, 'WatDiv benchmark generation' (the '12 committed watdiv-stress-*.yml' claim); duplicated in docs/benchmarks/WATDIV.md (## How to run line 59, and lines 193/240/326) · finder severity: low → verified: low*

- **Claim:** CLAUDE.md: "the 12 committed `watdiv-stress-*.yml` files are produced by `scripts/watdiv-preprocess/` (Python)." WATDIV.md line 59: "12 YAML files at `benchmarks/watdiv-stress-*.yml` reference Parquet artefacts hosted on ZivaHub."
- **Reality:** There are 13 committed files matching `benchmarks/watdiv-stress-*.yml`, not 12. The 13th is `watdiv-stress-default.yml`, a generator-driven benchmark (it has a `generator:` block, not `relations:`) added later and NOT produced by `scripts/watdiv-preprocess/`. The '12 produced by the preprocessor' count is correct for the static ZivaHub-backed snapshots, but the phrasing 'the 12 committed watdiv-stress-*.yml files' / '12 YAML files at benchmarks/watdiv-stress-*.yml' undercounts the glob by one. WATDIV.md itself documents `watdiv-stress-default` separately (lines 29-31), so the doc is self-aware but the literal count statements are off.
- **Evidence:** `git ls-files benchmarks/ | grep watdiv-stress` returns 13 files including benchmarks/watdiv-stress-default.yml; benchmarks/watdiv-stress-default.yml (lines 1-5) is a `generator: { kind: watdiv, scale: 10 }` block with no `relations:`; the other 12 contain `relations:` (static). Added in commit ebf97af 'feat(bench): add watdiv-basic and watdiv-stress-default benchmarks'.
- **Suggested fix:** Reword to 'the 12 preprocessor-produced static `watdiv-stress-*` snapshots' (distinguishing them from the generator-driven `watdiv-stress-default.yml`), or in WATDIV.md line 59 say 'The 12 static snapshot YAMLs (`watdiv-stress-{100,1000}-*.yml`)' rather than counting the whole `watdiv-stress-*.yml` glob.
- **Verifier note:** Verified against the repo. `git ls-files benchmarks/ | grep watdiv-stress` returns 13 files; the 13th, benchmarks/watdiv-stress-default.yml (lines 1-5), is a `generator: { kind: watdiv, scale: 10 }` block with no `relations:`, added in commit ebf97af, and is NOT produced by scripts/watdiv-preprocess/. The other 12 (watdiv-stress-{100,1000}-warmup + -test-{1..5}) are static ZivaHub-backed snapshots with `relations:` (confirmed: benchmarks/watdiv-stress-100-test-1.yml has `relations:` with zivahub URLs). So the count "12" is semantically correct (12 preprocessor-produced / ZivaHub-backed snapshots), but it is paired with the literal glob token `watdiv-stress-*.yml`, which now matches 13 files. Affected spots: CLAUDE.md:201 ("the 12 committed `watdiv-stress-*.yml` files are produced by `scripts/watdiv-preprocess/`"), docs/benchmarks/WATDIV.md:59, :193, :240, :326. This is a genuine glob-vs-count mismatch a verifying reader would notice, hence confirmed. Severity is correctly low (borderline trivial): every instance carries a disambiguating qualifier that correctly excludes the generator file — WATDIV.md:59 says "reference Parquet artefacts hosted on ZivaHub" (true of exactly 12), :240 says "snapshots", and WATDIV.md:29-31 explicitly defines watdiv-stress-default as distinct from "the frozen watdiv-stress-{100,1000} snapshots". The finder slightly overstates the harm since the docs are internally self-aware, but the literal "12 ... watdiv-stress-*.yml" phrasing does undercount the glob by one. Suggested fix (reword to "the 12 preprocessor-produced static watdiv-stress-{100,1000}-* snapshots") is appropriate.

Verified against kermit/src/main.rs: `enum JoinAlgorithmSelector {` is declared at line 153 (its `expand()` at line 159-160), 29 lines below the cited line 124. ### README.md — factual-drift
*unit: readme-root · location: "Analysis and plotting" section, line 149 · finder severity: medium → verified: low*

- **Claim:** "Six plot shapes return inline `matplotlib` Figures; pivot/comparison/stats helpers ship alongside."
- **Reality:** There are seven preset plot shapes, each returning a `matplotlib.figure.Figure`: scaling, bar_time, bar_space, tradeoff, dist, bar_queries, and ablation. `ablation` is a first-class preset (exported in `__all__`, has its own CLI subparser, and is listed in CLAUDE.md as `kl.ablation()`).
- **Evidence:** python/kermit-lab/kermit_lab/presets.py:17,23,31,36,41,49,64 define exactly seven preset functions (scaling, bar_time, bar_space, tradeoff, dist, bar_queries, ablation), all annotated `-> Figure`; python/kermit-lab/kermit_lab/__init__.py:27-49 `__all__` exports all seven; CLAUDE.md "No Criterion auto-plots" gotcha lists `kl.ablation()` among the presets.
- **Suggested fix:** Change "Six plot shapes" to "Seven plot shapes" (scaling, bar-time, bar-space, tradeoff, dist, bar-queries, ablation), or rephrase to avoid a hard count.
- **Verifier note:** README.md:149 states "Six plot shapes return inline matplotlib Figures" but there are seven. python/kermit-lab/kermit_lab/presets.py defines exactly seven functions each annotated `-> Figure`: scaling (line 17), bar_time (23), bar_space (31), tradeoff (36), dist (41), bar_queries (49), ablation (64). python/kermit-lab/kermit_lab/__init__.py exports all seven in `__all__` (lines 16-24, 27-49). `ablation` is genuinely first-class, not an internal helper: it has its own CLI subparser at drivers/main.py:90 (dispatch at 125-126), is called by render-all (drivers/render_all.py:86-90), and is documented as a preset in both CLAUDE.md:194 (`kl.ablation()`) and python/kermit-lab/README.md:47,70,111,124. The finder's line citations are all accurate. The count is a stale off-by-one (ablation was added after the prose was written) — note README.md:162's CLI list also omits `ablation`, listing only six subcommands, corroborating the drift. Severity downgraded from medium to low: it is a wrong-by-one numeric count in prose, low-consequence (the linked kermit-lab README lists the correct seven), not a workflow- or data-breaking error. Fix: change "Six" to "Seven" (add ablation) at line 149, and ideally add `ablation` to the CLI list at line 162.

### README.md — incompleteness
*unit: readme-root · location: "Analysis and plotting" section, line 162 · finder severity: low → verified: low*

- **Claim:** "... and CLI subcommands (`scaling`, `bar-time`, `bar-space`, `tradeoff`, `dist`, `bar-queries`)."
- **Reality:** The `kermit-lab` CLI also defines `plot` (the general engine), `ablation`, and `render-all` subcommands. `render-all` in particular is the subcommand the README itself demonstrates two lines earlier (line 159), so omitting it from the subcommand list is internally inconsistent. `ablation` and `plot` are likewise omitted.
- **Evidence:** python/kermit-lab/kermit_lab/drivers/main.py:51-100 registers subparsers: plot (51), scaling (64), bar-time (68), bar-space (73), tradeoff (76), dist (80), bar-queries (84), ablation (90), render-all (95). README.md:159 uses `kermit-lab render-all`.
- **Suggested fix:** Add `plot`, `ablation`, and `render-all` to the subcommand list (or note the list is illustrative), so it matches `main.py` and is consistent with the `render-all` example on line 159.
- **Verifier note:** Confirmed. README.md:162 lists CLI subcommands as exactly "(`scaling`, `bar-time`, `bar-space`, `tradeoff`, `dist`, `bar-queries`)" — six entries. The actual CLI in python/kermit-lab/kermit_lab/drivers/main.py registers nine subparsers: `plot` (line 51), `scaling` (64), `bar-time` (68), `bar-space` (73), `tradeoff` (76), `dist` (80), `bar-queries` (84), `ablation` (90), `render-all` (95). So `plot`, `ablation`, and `render-all` are omitted from README.md:162. The internal inconsistency is real: README.md:159 demonstrates `uv run kermit-lab render-all ...`, yet `render-all` is absent from the subcommand list three lines below at 162. The authoritative sub-README that line 162 points to (python/kermit-lab/README.md:104-124) correctly documents all nine subcommands, so a reader who follows the pointer recovers the full list — this caps the harm. Severity stays low: it is a pointer sentence whose parenthetical reads as illustrative and the deferred-to doc is complete, but the omission of `render-all` while the same page demonstrates it is a genuine, reader-visible inconsistency. Finder's evidence (line numbers, asserted reality, suggested fix) is accurate.

### USAGE.md — factual-drift
*unit: usage · location: Section 'End-to-end: scaling plot from scratch', line 409 (inline comment) · finder severity: low → verified: low*

- **Claim:** # 3. Run the triangle query at each scale, against both DS implementations.
- **Reality:** There are three index-structure implementations (ColumnTrie, HashTrie, TreeTrie), not two. The loop in the example only iterates `tree-trie column-trie`, so the prose 'both DS implementations' both undercounts the available structures and mismatches the broader system.
- **Evidence:** kermit-ds/src/ds/mod.rs:11-19 (`enum IndexStructure { ColumnTrie, HashTrie, TreeTrie }`); the example loop at USAGE.md:411 iterates only `for ds in tree-trie column-trie`.
- **Suggested fix:** Reword to 'against the two sorted-family DS implementations (tree-trie and column-trie)' to make clear the example deliberately omits hash-trie (which requires hash-triejoin), rather than implying only two structures exist.
- **Verifier note:** Both citations verified. USAGE.md:409 reads "# 3. Run the triangle query at each scale, against both DS implementations." and the loop at USAGE.md:411 iterates only `for ds in tree-trie column-trie`. kermit-ds/src/ds/mod.rs:11-19 confirms three IndexStructure variants exist (ColumnTrie, HashTrie, TreeTrie), so "both" undercounts the system's structures. The finding is genuine but minor, and the finder's diagnosis of the cause is correct: the example pins `-a leapfrog-triejoin`, and hash-trie is LFTJ-incompatible — per docs/algorithms/hash-triejoin.md:101 and docs/data-structures/hash-trie.md:129 HashTrie/HashTriejoin only consume each other, and the project memory (project_bench_run_all_all_panics.md:10-14) documents that (tree-trie|column-trie, HashTriejoin) and (hash-trie, leapfrog-triejoin) panic at db.rs:313. So the example is correct to use exactly two structures; only the prose word "both" is imprecise about why hash-trie is absent. Mitigating: USAGE.md uses "both DS variants/implementations" as an internal convention for the two LFTJ-compatible structures elsewhere (line 63 frames `--indexstructure` as "tree-trie ... or column-trie"; line 167 "Sweep both DS variants"), and the loop visibly names the two structures, so a reader is only weakly misled. Real clarity nit, correctly rated low; the finder's suggested rewording ("the two sorted-family DS implementations (tree-trie and column-trie)") is a sound fix. (Note: a stronger but out-of-scope bug exists at USAGE.md:167 — "Sweep both DS variants" describes `-i all`, which expands to all THREE structures and would attempt the panicking (hash-trie, leapfrog-triejoin) pair; not part of this finding.)

### benchmarks/README.md — incompleteness
*unit: bench-readmes · location: ## File layout, lines 7-9 · finder severity: low → verified: low*

- **Claim:** - `triangle.yml` is the minimal working example. / - `oxford-uniform-s*.yml` and `oxford-zipf-s*.yml` are realistic multi-relation benchmarks from the Oxford DSI dataset.
- **Reality:** The benchmarks/ directory also contains generator-driven example YAMLs that directly illustrate the generator schema this README documents at length — none are mentioned in File layout: lubm-example.yml, lubm-reference.yml, watdiv-example.yml, watdiv-basic.yml, watdiv-stress-default.yml (plus the 12 frozen watdiv-stress-* snapshots, which are covered later under "Frozen WatDiv snapshots").
- **Evidence:** Directory listing of benchmarks/ shows lubm-example.yml, lubm-reference.yml, watdiv-example.yml, watdiv-basic.yml, watdiv-stress-default.yml present; benchmarks/lubm-example.yml:1-6 and benchmarks/watdiv-example.yml:1-11 are generator specs. benchmarks/README.md:7-9 only enumerates triangle + oxford files.
- **Suggested fix:** Add a bullet pointing to the generator example files (e.g. "`watdiv-example.yml` / `lubm-example.yml` are minimal generator-driven examples; `watdiv-basic.yml` and `watdiv-stress-default.yml` are committed generator specs"), or soften the section to note it lists only representative files.
- **Verifier note:** Verified against benchmarks/README.md and the actual directory. The "File layout" section (benchmarks/README.md:7-9) is structured as a categorized inventory of the directory's files but enumerates only the static examples: triangle.yml (line 8) and oxford-uniform-s*/oxford-zipf-s* (line 9). git ls-files confirms 5 additional committed generator-driven YAMLs that this section omits: watdiv-example.yml (kind: watdiv, scale 1), lubm-example.yml (kind: lubm, queries [q1,q3]), lubm-reference.yml (kind: lubm), watdiv-stress-default.yml (kind: watdiv, scale 10), and watdiv-basic.yml (kind: watdiv-basic). These are a distinct category from the static examples and are NOT captured by the later "Frozen WatDiv snapshots (watdiv-stress-*.yml)" section (line 124) — that section describes the 2.4 MB frozen Python-produced snapshots, whereas watdiv-stress-default.yml is a 160-byte declarative generator spec (and would actually be mischaracterized if read into that pattern). So a reader using "File layout" as the directory map is not told that runnable generator examples exist. This is a real incompleteness. Severity stays low because (a) the section contains no false statement and reads as orientation, and (b) the generator schema and inline watdiv/lubm examples are documented at length later in the same README (lines 36-122). Note: watdiv-basic.yml's kind: watdiv-basic is a third generator kind documented in docs/benchmarks/WATDIV.md:25-27 but absent from the README's schema table (line 42 lists only watdiv | lubm) — a related gap, though outside the cited lines. The finder's evidence is accurate; the suggested-fix prose loosely calls watdiv-stress-default.yml a frozen-adjacent "committed generator spec," but that wording does not change the verdict.

### docs/algorithms/TEMPLATE.md — factual-drift
*unit: doc-templates · location: ## See also, line 38 · finder severity: medium → verified: low*

- **Claim:** `define_multiway_join_test_suite!` (...) — combinatorial coverage; this algorithm must pass all 11 patterns under every index structure (Priorities item 1).
- **Reality:** Symmetric to the DS-template issue: an algorithm only accepts data structures satisfying its JoinAlgo<DS> bound. HashTriejoin (DS: HashTrieIterable) can run only against HashTrie, not against TreeTrie/ColumnTrie (which are TrieIterable). 'Under every index structure' is therefore impossible for HashTriejoin. The real algorithm doc states this correctly — hash-triejoin.md's See-also says 'must pass all 11 patterns under `HashTrie`'. CLAUDE.md Priorities item 1 frames the obligation as 'under every compatible algorithm'. The template's generic 'every index structure' drops the compatibility qualifier.
- **Evidence:** kermit-algos/src/hash_triejoin.rs:251-253 (DS: HashTrieIterable); kermit-algos/src/leapfrog_triejoin.rs:448-450 (DS: TrieIterable); kermit-ds/src/ds/hash_trie/implementation.rs:199 (HashTrie implements HashTrieIterable, not TrieIterable); kermit/src/main.rs:119-141 (supports_algorithm lists hash-trie+hash-triejoin and tree/column-trie+leapfrog-triejoin as the only valid pairs); docs/algorithms/hash-triejoin.md:103 ('under `HashTrie`'); CLAUDE.md Priorities item 1
- **Suggested fix:** Change 'under every index structure' to 'under every compatible index structure', consistent with hash-triejoin.md and CLAUDE.md Priorities item 1.
- **Verifier note:** CONFIRMED. docs/algorithms/TEMPLATE.md:38 says an algorithm "must pass all 11 patterns under every index structure". This is false for hash-family algorithms because of JoinAlgo<DS> bounds. Evidence: kermit-algos/src/hash_triejoin.rs:251-253 bounds HashTriejoin on `DS: HashTrieIterable`; kermit-algos/src/leapfrog_triejoin.rs:448-450 bounds LeapfrogTriejoin on `DS: TrieIterable`. kermit-ds/src/ds/hash_trie/implementation.rs:199 shows HashTrie implements HashTrieIterable only (grep finds no TrieIterable impl), while kermit-ds/src/ds/tree_trie/tree_trie_iter.rs:163 and column_trie/column_trie_iter.rs:204 implement TrieIterable only. So HashTriejoin can never run against TreeTrie/ColumnTrie, and LeapfrogTriejoin can never run against HashTrie. kermit/src/main.rs:128-143 (supports_algorithm) encodes exactly two valid pairs, and the live test suite kermit/tests/join_tests.rs:12-18 pairs HashTrie variants ONLY with HashTriejoin — empirically, no algorithm is tested "under every index structure." The real component docs already state the correct qualified version: hash-triejoin.md:103 says "under `HashTrie`", and CLAUDE.md Priorities item 1 says "under every compatible algorithm." The template drops the compatibility qualifier. Severity lowered from medium to low: this is a TEMPLATE skeleton, not a shipped component doc; the concrete docs (hash-triejoin.md, leapfrog-triejoin.md) and CLAUDE.md are already correct, so the only reader who could be misled is a future author copying the skeleton (and the finding notes the DS template has the symmetric issue). Suggested fix — "under every compatible index structure" — is correct.

### docs/algorithms/hash-triejoin.md — factual-drift
*unit: algo-htj · location: ## Worked micro-example, lines 66-97 (cross-reference at line 97) · finder severity: low → verified: low*

- **Claim:** The worked example uses "R = {(1, 2)}", "S = {(2, 3), (2, 4)}", "T = {(1, 3), (1, 4)}" producing "Output (sorted): `[[1, 2, 3], [1, 2, 4]]`", and closes with "Corresponding test case: `join_algo_triangle` in [`kermit-algos/src/hash_triejoin.rs`]".
- **Reality:** The test named `join_algo_triangle` uses entirely different input data — a cyclic triangle where each relation has three tuples — and asserts a different output: `r = {(1,2),(2,3),(3,1)}`, `s = {(2,3),(3,1),(1,2)}`, `t = {(1,3),(2,1),(3,2)}`, expecting `out == [[1,2,3],[2,3,1],[3,1,2]]`. The test does verify the same triangle query shape but does NOT correspond to the data, trace, or output walked through in the doc, so a reader cannot use it to confirm the hand-trace. (The doc's TEMPLATE.md, line 33, specifically asks to "Link to a corresponding test case if one exists.")
- **Evidence:** kermit-algos/src/hash_triejoin.rs:378-391 (test `join_algo_triangle`: lines 380-382 define the 3-tuple cyclic relations; line 390 asserts `out == [[1,2,3],[2,3,1],[3,1,2]]`). Doc data/output at docs/algorithms/hash-triejoin.md:68-70 and line 95.
- **Suggested fix:** Either (a) change the doc's worked-example relations and expected output to match `join_algo_triangle` (the cyclic triangle producing 3 results), or (b) reword line 97 to stop implying data correspondence, e.g. "`join_algo_triangle` exercises the same triangle-query path under `HashTriejoin` (with different sample data)", or add a dedicated test whose data matches the trace.
- **Verifier note:** Confirmed. docs/algorithms/hash-triejoin.md:66-95 walks through a triangle trace with R={(1,2)}, S={(2,3),(2,4)}, T={(1,3),(1,4)} producing Output `[[1,2,3],[1,2,4]]` (2 results), then line 97 says "Corresponding test case: `join_algo_triangle`". But that test (kermit-algos/src/hash_triejoin.rs:378-391) uses entirely different data — a cyclic triangle r={(1,2),(2,3),(3,1)}, s={(2,3),(3,1),(1,2)}, t={(1,3),(2,1),(3,2)} (lines 380-382) — and asserts `[[1,2,3],[2,3,1],[3,1,2]]` (line 390, 3 results). The query shape `Q(X,Y,Z) :- R(X,Y),S(Y,Z),T(X,Z).` is identical (hash_triejoin.rs:383 vs doc line 66), and `join_algo_triangle` is the only triangle test in the file (grep shows only it and `join_algo_unary_intersection`), so no alternate test matches the trace. The data, intermediate trace, and result set in the doc cannot be confirmed by the cited test, so "Corresponding test case" over-claims. Severity correctly rated low: the algorithm/query/path are accurate and the linked test does exercise the same triangle path under HashTriejoin — only the sample data and result count diverge, and TEMPLATE.md:33 merely asks to "Link to a corresponding test case if one exists," which is loosely met. A reader expecting the test to verify the hand-trace would be mildly misled. Fix (b) — rewording line 97 to drop the data-correspondence implication — is the minimal correct remedy.

### docs/algorithms/leapfrog-triejoin.md — factual-drift
*unit: algo-lftj · location: Invariants, line 44 — 'if it returns `false` the triejoin asserts and panics ([leapfrog_triejoin.rs:251]...)' · finder severity: medium → verified: low*

- **Claim:** `up()` on an active iterator must succeed; if it returns `false` the triejoin asserts and panics ([leapfrog_triejoin.rs:251](../../kermit-algos/src/leapfrog_triejoin.rs))
- **Reality:** Line 251 is `return false;` — the depth==0 no-op early return of `triejoin_up` (the opposite of a panic). The assert that actually panics on a failed `up()` is `assert!(iter.up(), ...)` at lines 254-257.
- **Evidence:** kermit-algos/src/leapfrog_triejoin.rs:251 (`return false;`, inside `if self.depth == 0`); kermit-algos/src/leapfrog_triejoin.rs:254-257 (`assert!(\n    iter.up(),\n    "iterator must be able to move up from non-root depth (LFTJ invariant)"\n);`)
- **Suggested fix:** Change `leapfrog_triejoin.rs:251` to `leapfrog_triejoin.rs:254` so it points at the `assert!(iter.up(), ...)` call.
- **Verifier note:** Confirmed line-citation drift. docs/algorithms/leapfrog-triejoin.md:44 states "if it returns `false` the triejoin asserts and panics ([leapfrog_triejoin.rs:251]...)". But kermit-algos/src/leapfrog_triejoin.rs:251 is `return false;` — the depth==0 graceful no-op inside `if self.depth == 0 {` (lines 250-252), documented at line 239 as "Returns `false` (no-op) when already at the root." That is the opposite of a panic. The assert that actually panics on a failed `up()` is at lines 254-257: `assert!(iter.up(), "iterator must be able to move up from non-root depth (LFTJ invariant)")`. So the cited line points at the most misleading possible target — the very "graceful return false" the prose contrasts against. The finder's reality and suggested fix (251 -> 254) are accurate. Severity lowered from medium to low: the prose is conceptually correct (a failed up() does panic via the assert); only the line pointer is wrong (off by 3), so it misleads a reader verifying the citation but does not misrepresent the algorithm's behavior.

### docs/benchmarks/LUBM.md — internal-inconsistency
*unit: bench-lubm · location: ## Practical scale ceiling, scale table — line 186 · finder severity: low → verified: low*

- **Claim:** `| LUBM(50, 0) | ~6.9 M | ~15 GB+ | Will OOM on most machines |`
- **Reality:** The implementation's own module docstring estimates roughly 1 GB peak at the same scale: "At LUBM(50) (~6.9M ABox triples) peak memory is ~1 GB." The two figures describe the identical in-memory entailment implementation yet differ by ~15x. (Both are unverified estimates — the implementation is gated on `java` and the scale is never run in CI — but they cannot both be correct.)
- **Evidence:** kermit-rdf/src/lubm/entailment.rs:26 ("At LUBM(50) (~6.9M ABox triples) peak memory is ~1 GB."); the working set is a single HashSet plus one per-iteration Vec clone, kermit-rdf/src/lubm/entailment.rs:344 and :375
- **Suggested fix:** Reconcile the two estimates: either lower the LUBM(50,0) row to the ~1 GB order of magnitude the entailment.rs docstring claims (and likewise re-scale the LUBM(5,0)/LUBM(10,0) rows), or, if ~15 GB+ is the measured truth, correct the stale ~1 GB figure in entailment.rs:26. Whichever number is empirically right, make the two locations agree.
- **Verifier note:** Both citations verified verbatim and the discrepancy is real. docs/benchmarks/LUBM.md:186 states `| LUBM(50, 0) | ~6.9 M | ~15 GB+ | Will OOM on most machines |`, while the module docstring at kermit-rdf/src/lubm/entailment.rs:25 says "At LUBM(50) (~6.9M ABox triples) peak memory is ~1 GB." Both describe the peak memory of the same in-memory entailment routine `entail()` over the same ~6.9M-triple LUBM(50) closure, so they cannot both be order-of-magnitude correct (~15x apart). I confirmed the working-set shape the finder asserts by reading the implementation: a single `HashSet<(String,String,RdfValue)>` named `all` (entailment.rs:344) holding the full closure, plus one per-iteration `Vec` clone `snap` (entailment.rs:375) — peak ≈ 2x the closed set. A repo-wide grep found no third source reconciling the two figures. A secondary inconsistency in the same pair of sentences: the doc labels ~6.9 M as post-entailment triples while entailment.rs:25 labels it pre-entailment ABox triples. Severity correctly low: both numbers are explicitly unverified estimates, the path is `java`-gated and never run in CI (Tests table at LUBM.md:209-215; "Streaming entailment ... future work" at LUBM.md:238), and no code/test depends on either value — the only harm is misleading a reader about RAM needed for a never-run scale. Finder's suggested fix (make the two locations agree on whichever number is empirically right) is sound.

### docs/benchmarks/LUBM.md — factual-drift
*unit: bench-lubm · location: ## Workload reference, intro paragraph — line 136-137 · finder severity: low → verified: low*

- **Claim:** "URIs reference `Department0.University0.edu`, which exists in any LUBM(N ≥ 1) by the data generator's deterministic 0-indexed naming, so queries are universal across all scales."
- **Reality:** Only some queries reference `http://www.Department0.University0.edu...` (Q1, Q3, Q4, Q5, Q7, Q10). Q8, Q11, Q12, and Q13 instead key off the university-level URI `http://www.University0.edu` (no Department0 segment). The doc states a single URI form as if it is the only constant the queries depend on.
- **Evidence:** kermit-rdf/queries/lubm/q11.sparql line 7 (`<http://www.University0.edu>`); q12.sparql line 9 (`<http://www.University0.edu>`); q13.sparql line 7 (`<http://www.University0.edu>`); q8.sparql line 9 (`<http://www.University0.edu>`); contrast q1.sparql line 6 (`<http://www.Department0.University0.edu/GraduateCourse0>`)
- **Suggested fix:** Broaden the sentence to name both deterministic constants, e.g. "URIs reference the 0-indexed entities `http://www.University0.edu` and `http://www.Department0.University0.edu`, both of which exist in any LUBM(N ≥ 1) ...".
- **Verifier note:** Confirmed real factual-drift, severity low. docs/benchmarks/LUBM.md:135-137 states "URIs reference `Department0.University0.edu`, which exists in any LUBM(N ≥ 1) ... so queries are universal across all scales," presenting that single URI form as the constant the queries depend on. Verified against all 14 query files: 6 queries use the `Department0.University0.edu` form (q1.sparql:6, q3.sparql:6, q4.sparql:6, q5.sparql:6, q7.sparql:8, q10.sparql:6), but 4 queries key off the university-level `http://www.University0.edu` (no Department0 segment): q8.sparql:8, q11.sparql:6, q12.sparql:8, q13.sparql:6. (The remaining 4 — q2, q6, q9, q14 — reference no scale-specific entity URI at all, only rdf/ub prefixes.) This contradicts the intro prose and is internally inconsistent with the doc's own table, which describes Q8/Q11/Q13 as "scoped to University0" (LUBM.md:148,151,153). Two qualifiers keep this at low: (1) the sentence's actual conclusion — scale-universality via deterministic 0-indexing — is still true, since both URI forms exist in any LUBM(N≥1); (2) the finder's evidence line numbers are each off by one (q11 is :6 not :7, q12 :8 not :9, q13 :6 not :7, q8 :8 not :9; only the q1.sparql:6 citation is exact), but the substance is correct. The suggested fix (name both `University0.edu` and `Department0.University0.edu`) is appropriate; ideally it would also acknowledge the 4 queries that reference neither.

### docs/benchmarks/WATDIV.md — factual-drift
*unit: bench-watdiv · location: Vendored generator section, line 253 · finder severity: medium → verified: low*

- **Claim:** `kermit-rdf/vendor/watdiv/bin/Release/watdiv` (~448 KB ...)
- **Reality:** The committed binary is 360112 bytes ≈ 351.7 KiB ≈ 360 KB, not ~448 KB.
- **Evidence:** stat -c %s kermit-rdf/vendor/watdiv/bin/Release/watdiv => 360112; git cat-file -s HEAD:kermit-rdf/vendor/watdiv/bin/Release/watdiv => 360112
- **Suggested fix:** Change '~448 KB' to '~360 KB' (or '~352 KiB').
- **Verifier note:** docs/benchmarks/WATDIV.md:253 says the vendored binary is "~448 KB". The actual file kermit-rdf/vendor/watdiv/bin/Release/watdiv is 360112 bytes both on disk (stat -c %s => 360112, ls -la confirms) and in git (git cat-file -s HEAD:... => 360112). 360112 bytes = 360.1 KB decimal = 351.7 KiB binary. Neither "448 KB" interpretation matches: 448 KB = 448000 bytes, 448 KiB = 458752 bytes — both ~24% larger than reality. The finder's evidence reproduces exactly and the suggested fix (~360 KB or ~352 KiB) is correct. Discrepancy is real factual drift, so confirmed. Downgraded from medium to low: this is a parenthetical approximate file size on a gitignored vendored tool, not a load-bearing fact — a reader would not make any wrong build/behavior decision from it. Note (out of scope for this finding): the same line also claims the binary is "gitignored / not committed", but in this worktree it is in fact tracked (git ls-files returns it, git check-ignore exit 1) — a separate potential finding not covered by the size claim under review.

### docs/benchmarks/WATDIV.md — factual-drift
*unit: bench-watdiv · location: How to run / Committed snapshots, line 59 · finder severity: low → verified: low*

- **Claim:** 12 YAML files at `benchmarks/watdiv-stress-*.yml` reference Parquet artefacts hosted on ZivaHub.
- **Reality:** The glob benchmarks/watdiv-stress-*.yml matches 13 files. Twelve reference ZivaHub Parquet artefacts (the warmup + test-1..5 at SF 100 and 1000); the 13th, benchmarks/watdiv-stress-default.yml, is a generator-block spec with no ZivaHub URLs. So the count '12' applies only to the ZivaHub-referencing subset, not the glob.
- **Evidence:** ls benchmarks/watdiv-stress-*.yml | wc -l => 13; grep -l 'generator:' benchmarks/watdiv-stress-*.yml => benchmarks/watdiv-stress-default.yml; per-file zivahub URL counts: 12 files = 61 each, watdiv-stress-default.yml = 0; benchmarks/watdiv-stress-default.yml head shows `generator: { kind: watdiv, scale: 10 }`
- **Suggested fix:** Either narrow the glob to the snapshot pattern (e.g. 'the 12 benchmarks/watdiv-stress-{100,1000}-{warmup,test-1..5}.yml files') or note that watdiv-stress-default.yml is a separate generator spec, not a ZivaHub snapshot.
- **Verifier note:** docs/benchmarks/WATDIV.md:59 says "12 YAML files at `benchmarks/watdiv-stress-*.yml` reference Parquet artefacts hosted on ZivaHub." Verified the glob `benchmarks/watdiv-stress-*.yml` matches 13 files, not 12 (`ls ... | wc -l` => 13). The 13th is benchmarks/watdiv-stress-default.yml, which is a generator spec (its full contents are just `name`, `description`, and `generator: { kind: watdiv, scale: 10 }`) with 0 ZivaHub references; the other 12 each contain 61 ZivaHub URL references (per-file grep -c -i zivahub). So the count "12" correctly describes the ZivaHub-referencing snapshot subset, but the glob it is attached to over-matches by one file — a real factual drift introduced when the generator file was added. Severity stays low because (a) the substantive claim (12 ZivaHub-backed snapshots) is accurate, (b) the table immediately following at lines 70-73 enumerates exactly those 12 files and correctly omits watdiv-stress-default.yml, and (c) the doc separately documents watdiv-stress-default at lines 29-31 as a distinct generator workload "complementing the frozen watdiv-stress-{100,1000} snapshots." The finder did not overstate the issue (they affirmed the count, only flagged the glob), and the suggested fix — narrow the glob to the snapshot pattern or note that watdiv-stress-default.yml is a separate generator spec — is correct.

- **Evidence:** docs/benchmarks/WATDIV.md:260; grep for the path in repo only finds docs/plans/2026-06-11-watdiv-default-workloads.md:41 (also a /tb/... machine-local path); no in-repo watdiv-cpp/ directory exists

The cited `/tb/Source/Academia/watdiv-rs/` is an author-machine-local absolute path (it is the parent of the audit worktree itself), not a repo artifact — it points outside the repository. grep confirms `watdiv-cpp` appears only in two docs: WATDIV.md:260 and docs/plans/2026-06-11-watdiv-default-workloads.md:41 (an even more machine-specific `/tb/.../watdiv-rs/.loom/worktrees/aidanb/...` path). ### docs/benchmarks/WATDIV.md — incompleteness
*unit: bench-watdiv · location: Pipeline ASCII diagram, line 159 · finder severity: low → verified: low*

- **Claim:** `-q <model> <template> <count>` (shown as the third watdiv invocation in the pipeline diagram)
- **Reality:** The driver invokes `-q` with FOUR positional args: `-q <model> <template-file> <count> <recurrence>`, with recurrence hard-coded to "1". The diagram omits the trailing recurrence argument. (CLAUDE.md's own gotcha documents the 4-arg form.)
- **Evidence:** kermit-rdf/src/driver/invoke.rs:194-207 (run_queries builds args ['-q', model, tpl, &count_str, recurrence] with `let recurrence = "1";`)
- **Suggested fix:** Update the diagram line to `-q <model> <template> <count> <recurrence=1>` to match the actual invocation.
- **Verifier note:** The discrepancy is real but minor. docs/benchmarks/WATDIV.md:159 shows the third watdiv invocation in the pipeline diagram as `-q <model> <template> <count>` (three positional args). The actual driver in kermit-rdf/src/driver/invoke.rs:201-207 (run_queries) passes FOUR positional args after `-q`: model, template, count, and `recurrence` (= "1", set at line 200). The same function's own doc comment (invoke.rs:188) and CLAUDE.md's WatDiv gotcha both document the 4-arg form `-q <model> <query-file> <count> <recurrence>`, confirming the diagram is the outlier. The sibling diagram lines (157 `-d <model> <scale>`, 158 `-s <model> <data> <q-size> <q-n>`) list all positional args, so the diagram is meant to be literal about positionals — the omission is a genuine inconsistency, not intentional abstraction. Severity remains low: it's a schematic overview, the omitted arg is a hard-coded constant the user cannot tune, and the authoritative spec is correctly stated elsewhere. Suggested fix (`-q <model> <template> <count> <recurrence=1>`) is appropriate.

### docs/data-structures/TEMPLATE.md — factual-drift
*unit: doc-templates · location: ## See also, line 49 · finder severity: medium → verified: low*

- **Claim:** `define_multiway_join_test_suite!` (...) — combinatorial coverage; this structure must pass all 11 patterns under every algorithm (Priorities item 1).
- **Reality:** A data structure can only be joined by algorithms whose JoinAlgo<DS> bound it satisfies, so 'every algorithm' is impossible, not merely untested. LeapfrogTriejoin is `impl<DS> JoinAlgo<DS> for LeapfrogTriejoin where DS: TrieIterable`; HashTriejoin is `impl<DS> JoinAlgo<DS> for HashTriejoin where DS: HashTrieIterable`. TreeTrie/ColumnTrie implement only TrieIterable; HashTrie implements only HashTrieIterable, so each structure works with exactly one of the two algorithms. The CLI encodes the same restriction in IndexStructureSelector::supports_algorithm, and the actual test wiring pairs only compatible combinations. CLAUDE.md Priorities item 1 itself says 'under every compatible algorithm', and the real component docs follow that (e.g. hash-trie.md: 'must pass all 11 patterns under `HashTriejoin`'). The template drops the word 'compatible'.
- **Evidence:** kermit-algos/src/leapfrog_triejoin.rs:448-450 (impl JoinAlgo for LeapfrogTriejoin where DS: TrieIterable); kermit-algos/src/hash_triejoin.rs:251-253 (impl JoinAlgo for HashTriejoin where DS: HashTrieIterable); kermit-ds/src/ds/tree_trie/tree_trie_iter.rs:163 and kermit-ds/src/ds/column_trie/column_trie_iter.rs:204 (impl TrieIterable only); kermit-ds/src/ds/hash_trie/implementation.rs:199 (impl HashTrieIterable only); kermit/src/main.rs:128-141 (supports_algorithm gate); kermit/tests/join_tests.rs:12-18 (TreeTrie/ColumnTrie with LeapfrogTriejoin, HashTrie* with HashTriejoin only); CLAUDE.md Priorities item 1 ('every compatible algorithm'); docs/data-structures/hash-trie.md:130
- **Suggested fix:** Change 'under every algorithm' to 'under every compatible algorithm' (matching CLAUDE.md Priorities item 1 and the wording the real DS docs use), so authors do not infer that a new structure must run against an algorithm whose trait bound it cannot satisfy.
- **Verifier note:** Doc claim confirmed verbatim at docs/data-structures/TEMPLATE.md:49: "this structure must pass all 11 patterns under every algorithm (Priorities item 1)." The asserted reality is accurate: algorithm applicability is a compile-time trait bound, not just test convention. kermit-algos/src/leapfrog_triejoin.rs:448-450 binds `LeapfrogTriejoin: JoinAlgo<DS> where DS: TrieIterable`; kermit-algos/src/hash_triejoin.rs:251-253 binds `HashTriejoin: JoinAlgo<DS> where DS: HashTrieIterable`. TreeTrie/ColumnTrie implement only TrieIterable (tree_trie_iter.rs:163, column_trie_iter.rs:204) and HashTrie implements only HashTrieIterable (hash_trie/implementation.rs:199), so each structure type-checks against exactly one algorithm. The CLI encodes the same gate (kermit/src/main.rs:128-143, "Anything else is incompatible") and the tests pair only compatible combos (kermit/tests/join_tests.rs:12-18). The authoritative phrasing uses "compatible": CLAUDE.md Priorities item 1 ("under every compatible algorithm") and the concrete doc hash-trie.md:130 ("under `HashTriejoin`"). The template drops "compatible," so it does misrepresent the contract. Severity lowered from medium to low: it is a single dropped qualifier in a skeleton template that self-corrects against the very item it cites (CLAUDE.md item 1) and against the existing DS docs that model the right wording; the trait bound makes any incorrect inference fail to compile, so there is no runtime/correctness consequence. Suggested fix (add "compatible") is correct.

### docs/data-structures/TEMPLATE.md — incompleteness
*unit: doc-templates · location: ## Complexity, complexity table rows, lines 29-33 · finder severity: low → verified: low*

- **Claim:** Table rows hardcode `TrieIterator::key()`, `TrieIterator::next()`, `TrieIterator::seek(target)`, `TrieIterator::open()`, `TrieIterator::up()` as the per-structure iterator operations to document.
- **Reality:** Hash-based structures expose the deliberately-separate HashTrieIterator trait, not TrieIterator. HashTrieIterator has no `seek` (hash navigation is exact-match, explicitly 'incompatible' with LinearIterator::seek) and adds `lookup`, `size`, and `leaf_tuples`. Consequently docs/data-structures/hash-trie.md could not use this table verbatim: it lists `HashTrieIterator::key/next/lookup/size/open/up/leaf_tuples`. The template gives no hash-trie variant or note, so it fits only the two sorted tries (tree-trie.md, column-trie.md).
- **Evidence:** kermit-iters/src/hash_trie.rs:34-38 ('Not a `LinearIterator` ... a separate trait family rather than an extension') and trait methods at kermit-iters/src/hash_trie.rs:39-70 (key/next/lookup/size/at_end/open/up/leaf_tuples — no seek); docs/data-structures/hash-trie.md:51-57 (HashTrieIterator::lookup/size/leaf_tuples rows); contrast docs/data-structures/tree-trie.md:42-46 and column-trie.md:50-53 which match the template
- **Suggested fix:** Add a note under the complexity table that hash-based structures document the HashTrieIterator surface instead (key/next/lookup/size/open/up/leaf_tuples, no seek), or provide an alternate row set for hash tries, so the template covers all three structure families.
- **Verifier note:** Verified all cited evidence independently. TEMPLATE.md lines 29-33 hardcode exactly five iterator rows (`TrieIterator::key/next/seek(target)/open/up`) in the Complexity table, with no hash-trie variant or note; line 50's "See also" also only references the `TrieIterable` contract. The hash family uses a deliberately separate trait: kermit-iters/src/hash_trie.rs:34-38 states "`LinearIterator::seek` ... and exact-match [hash] ... contracts are incompatible, so `HashTrieIterator` is a separate trait family rather than an extension," and the trait methods at lines 39-70 are key/next/lookup/size/at_end/open/up/leaf_tuples — there is NO `seek`, and it adds lookup/size/leaf_tuples. Consequently docs/data-structures/hash-trie.md:51-57 could not use the template verbatim: it lists `HashTrieIterator::key/next/lookup/size/open/up/leaf_tuples` (no seek). By contrast tree-trie.md:41-45 and column-trie.md:49-53 match the template's `TrieIterator` rows exactly. So the template fits only the two sorted tries and silently omits the hash-trie family. This is a genuine incompleteness in an author-facing skeleton, but it is not a factual misstatement about runtime behavior and the existing hash-trie.md already models the correct divergent row set, so "low" severity is accurate.

### docs/data-structures/tree-trie.md — internal-inconsistency
*unit: ds-tree · location: Complexity table, `insert(tuple)` row (line 39) vs. docs/data-structures/column-trie.md insert row (line 47) · finder severity: low → verified: low*

- **Claim:** tree-trie.md states `insert(tuple)` is `O(a · log b)` with note "binary search at each level; absorbs duplicates" (no mention of the sibling-shift), whereas column-trie.md (line 47) states `insert(tuple)` is `O(a · b) worst-case` and explicitly notes "insertions in early layers shift later-layer offsets".
- **Reality:** Both structures pay an O(b) shift on insert into a sorted sequence (TreeTrie via `Vec::insert(pos, new_node)` shifting siblings; ColumnTrie via `insert_key_and_shift_intervals`). The two sibling docs describe the same asymptotic class differently — column-trie's O(a·b) is the accurate one; tree-trie's O(a·log b) is the outlier.
- **Evidence:** kermit-ds/src/ds/tree_trie/implementation.rs:24 (`children.insert(pos, new_node)`); docs/data-structures/column-trie.md:47 (`O(a · b) worst-case` ... `insert_key_and_shift_intervals`)
- **Suggested fix:** Align tree-trie's `insert` row with column-trie's: report O(a · b) worst-case and note the shift, so the two pointer/column siblings are described consistently.
- **Verifier note:** CONFIRMED. The two sibling docs describe structurally identical sorted-array inserts with different asymptotic classes, and tree-trie's is the inaccurate outlier.  Doc claims verified verbatim: - docs/data-structures/tree-trie.md:39 — `insert(tuple)` | O(a · log b) | note "binary search at each level; absorbs duplicates" (no shift mentioned). - docs/data-structures/column-trie.md:47 — `insert(tuple)` | O(a · b) worst-case | note "insertions in early layers shift later-layer offsets (`insert_key_and_shift_intervals`)".  Code reality: - TreeTrie insert recurses one level per arity via `insert_into_children` (kermit-ds/src/ds/tree_trie/implementation.rs:11, called per level at :19). At each level it does `binary_search_by` (:17, genuinely O(log b)) but on a new key calls `children.insert(pos, new_node)` (:24). `children` is `Vec<TrieNode>` (:36), so this is `Vec::insert`, which shifts every sibling after `pos` — O(b) worst-case. Dominant per-level cost is therefore O(b), giving O(a · b) worst-case over `a` levels, not O(a · log b). (Independently, `key_iter.collect()` at :19/:23 rebuilds the remaining-key vec each level, an additional cost the table also omits.) - ColumnTrie's equivalent op `insert_key_and_shift_intervals` (kermit-ds/src/ds/column_trie/implementation.rs:57) does `data.insert(pos, key)` (:58) plus an interval-shift loop (:59-61) — the same O(b) `Vec::insert` shift. Its doc correctly reports O(a · b).  So both structures pay the same O(b) shift; column-trie's O(a · b) is accurate and tree-trie's O(a · log b) understates the true class by omitting the shift. This corroborates the finder. The tree-trie `from_tuples` row (:40, O(n · a · log n)) likewise omits the shift term that column-trie's row (:48) includes, showing the omission is systematic — further evidence, not a refutation.  This misleads a reader comparing the two structures (the explicit thesis purpose), suggesting TreeTrie inserts are asymptotically cheaper than ColumnTrie when both are O(a · b) worst-case.  Severity left at low (finder's rating is defensible): it is a docs-table asymptotic mislabel, not a runtime defect, and the table's own `seek` row (tree-trie.md:43, O(b) linear scan, flagged at :48) already signals the table isn't uniformly optimistic. Borders on medium only because of the comparative-thesis context, not enough to override. Suggested fix (align tree-trie's insert row to O(a · b) worst-case with a shift note) is correct.
