**ds-hash** (2 confirmed / 2 raw) — docs: docs/data-structures/hash-trie.md

The hash-trie doc is largely accurate: the representation (nested HashTable nodes, Inner/Leaf split, Entry{hash,value}, bucket index `hash >> (64 - log2_capacity)`, linear probing, full-64-bit-hash storage), the 0.7 load-factor cap with doubling-and-rehash, multiset semantics with full tuples in leaf chains, the complexity table (all method names match the `HashTrieIterator` trait in kermit-iters/src/hash_trie.rs), the worked example, the CLI surface (`-i hash-trie`, `--ds-layout-hasher sip|fxhash`, default sip), the Layout option mapping to `HashTrie<H: HashStrategy>` / `ds_layout_hasher`, and the `HashTrieSip`/`HashTrieFx` test aliases all check out against the code. The one substantive problem is Invariant #2 (\"Hash function consistency\"): it describes a non-existent function `hash_attribute(depth, key)` \"exported from kermit-iters\" with a depth parameter \"preserved for ABI compatibility,\" allegedly imported by both `HashTrie::insert_at` and `SingletonHashTrieIter::new`. No such function exists anywhere in the repo; the real mechanism is the single-argument `HashStrategy::hash(key)` selected by the type parameter `H`, and the singleton takes a precomputed hash rather than importing any function. This is stale pre-refactor prose that also internally contradicts the doc's own (correct) Optimizations/Layout section. Two findings filed; both concern this invariant.


**ds-tree** (2 confirmed / 2 raw) — docs: docs/data-structures/tree-trie.md

The tree-trie.md doc is largely accurate: the representation pseudo-Rust matches the actual `TreeTrie`/`TrieNode` field layout (implementation.rs:34-37, 99-102), the invariants (path depth = arity, sorted/dedup siblings, forward-only seek that panics on `seek_key < key`, the LFTJ open-after-at_end discipline) all match the code (tree_trie_iter.rs:90-119, 131-147), the CLI name `-i tree-trie` is correct (derived from the `TreeTrie` clap ValueEnum variant in kermit/src/main.rs:105), the HeapSize heap-only claim is correct (implementation.rs:197-207 sums Vec capacities only), and the worked micro-example iteration trace is correct step-for-step against the iterator. The one substantive issue is the `insert(tuple)` time complexity: the doc reports O(a · log b) and notes only the binary search, but the code's `Vec::insert(pos, new_node)` shift makes it O(a · b) worst-case — the same shift cost that the sibling column-trie.md doc correctly reports, creating both a factual-drift and an internal-inconsistency between the two docs. (`from_tuples`, which sorts first and thus appends, remains correctly stated.) No broken references, missing template sections, or stale content found.

**readme-algos** (4 confirmed / 4 raw) — docs: kermit-algos/README.md

kermit-algos/README.md is structurally fine — every file-path link and the ARCHITECTURE.md#leapfrog-triejoin anchor resolve correctly, and the JoinAlgo trait description matches join_algo.rs — but it is materially stale. The README predates the HashTriejoin algorithm and presents the crate as implementing only the Leapfrog Triejoin ("Currently implements the Leapfrog Triejoin", line 3). In reality the crate ships two JoinAlgo implementations: LeapfrogTriejoin (over TrieIterable) and HashTriejoin (over HashTrieIterable, hash_triejoin.rs:251), with both surfaced as JoinAlgorithm enum variants (lib.rs:36-42) and CLI-wired. HashTriejoin is absent from the intro, the entry-points list, and the relationship section (which also omits HashTrie as an accepted in-practice structure). The "Extending" recipe is correct but thinner than the actual contract in CLAUDE.md. No broken references were found; the issues are all about the missing second algorithm.

README.md is broadly well-structured and most of its file/link references, build/run commands, and benchmark-name claims (the oxford-* and watdiv-stress-* suites) check out. Smaller drifts: \"Six plot shapes\" should be seven (ablation is a real preset), the declarative-generator examples `watdiv-100`/`lubm-1` (line 64) are not real benchmark names, and the Python CLI subcommand list (line 162) omits `plot`/`ablation`/`render-all` — notably `render-all`, which the README itself demonstrates one line earlier.

**rust-rdf** (3 confirmed / 3 raw) — docs: kermit-rdf/src/lib.rs, kermit-rdf/src/pipeline.rs, kermit-rdf/src/partition.rs, kermit-rdf/src/parquet.rs, kermit-rdf/src/dict.rs, kermit-rdf/src/expected.rs, kermit-rdf/src/yaml_emit.rs, kermit-rdf/src/ntriples.rs, kermit-rdf/src/value.rs, kermit-rdf/src/timestamp.rs, kermit-rdf/src/error.rs, kermit-rdf/src/driver/mod.rs, kermit-rdf/src/driver/invoke.rs, kermit-rdf/src/driver/sandbox.rs, kermit-rdf/src/lubm/mod.rs, kermit-rdf/src/lubm/driver.rs, kermit-rdf/src/lubm/entailment.rs, kermit-rdf/src/lubm/pipeline.rs, kermit-rdf/src/lubm/queries.rs, kermit-rdf/src/lubm/sandbox.rs, kermit-rdf/src/sparql/mod.rs, kermit-rdf/src/sparql/parser.rs, kermit-rdf/src/sparql/translator.rs, kermit-rdf/src/sparql/bindings.rs

The kermit-rdf in-source documentation is largely accurate and unusually self-aware: the driver invoke comments correctly state watdiv writes only to stdout and emits no .desc sidecars; the #end-splitting, bwrap heavy-recipe, gunzip self-triple filter, entailment rule list, and yaml_emit/generator handling all match the code precisely, and the LUBM reference cardinalities in queries.rs agree with docs/benchmarks/LUBM.md. Three discrepancies were found, all low-to-medium severity: (1) entailment.rs points to `tests/lubm_cardinalities.rs` which does not exist in this crate's tests/ dir — it lives in `kermit/tests/`, as the sibling lubm/README.md correctly notes; (2) the crate-level lib.rs doc still describes only the WatDiv pipeline and omits the entire LUBM pipeline that is now a first-class module; (3) the pipeline.rs output-layout diagram lists `*.desc` files that the vendored binary never produces, contradicting the sibling expected.rs and invoke.rs docs. None affect correctness of the code, but all could misdirect a reader.


**spec-bench-report** (1 confirmed / 1 raw) — docs: docs/specs/bench-report-schema.md

docs/specs/bench-report-schema.md is highly accurate against its stated source of truth (kermit/src/bench_report.rs) and the call sites in kermit/src/main.rs. Verified correct: schema_version = 2 (bench_report.rs:68); the complete BenchReport field catalogue (schema_version:u32, kind:BenchKind, metadata:Vec<ReportField> of label/value strings, axes:BTreeMap<String, serde_json::Value>, criterion_groups:Vec<CriterionGroupRef>); CriterionGroupRef fields (group/function/metric); lowercase serialization of kind (join/ds/run) and metric (time/space); the default path bench-runs/<kind>-<unix-millis>.json and the --report-json override (main.rs:566-576, 268-272); pretty-printed JSON-array output (write_json_report, bench_report.rs:167-168); and the entire conventional-axes \"Populated by\" table, which matches all four axes-construction call sites exactly (join: data_structure/algorithm/relations at main.rs:1432; ds: data_structure/relation_path/relation_bytes/tuples/arity at :733 and :852; run: benchmark/query/data_structure/algorithm/tuples at :1039 and :1215). The Criterion-side claims (estimates.json fields incl. slope as Option->null, sample.json {sampling_mode,iters,times}, benchmark.json 7-field shape incl. directory_name, tukey.json, base/new layout, slash-to-underscore directory flattening) all check out against the actually-used criterion 0.7.0. The optimization-standard.md cross-reference resolves, and the change log matches git history (axes added in the v2 bump commit ac52ba4). The only issue is a single low-severity overstatement on line 8 (\"regardless of subcommand\"), which the rest of the doc already implicitly contradicts.


**spec-benchmarking-arch** (6 confirmed / 6 raw) — docs: docs/specs/benchmarking-architecture.md

The doc is broadly accurate on the per-subcommand mechanics it does cover: BenchArgs flag names/defaults (sample-size=100, measurement-time=5s, warm-up-time=3s), default Criterion group names (join/ds, run/...), the bench ds function names ({ds}/insertion|iteration|space), the bench run function names (insertion/iteration/space/{rel}), the cache path (~/.cache/kermit/benchmarks/), the bench join iter_batched flow, format_bytes/SpaceMeasurement/BytesFormatter locations, write_metadata_block living in bench_report, and the zero-internal-deps + miri-exclusion claims for kermit-bench. However, the doc predates the on-the-fly benchmark generator and the always-on JSON report, so it is materially stale in several places: it omits the seventh subcommand bench gen entirely (Overview claims only six), it describes the bench run resolution flow using the wrong discovery functions and omits the materialize step that runs for generator benchmarks, the kermit-bench module tree omits GeneratorSpec and the generator field, the --report-json table entry implies no report is written by default when in fact one is always emitted to bench-runs/, and it contains an internal inconsistency claiming the bench run iteration function name is shared with bench ds when one is bare `iteration` and the other is `{ds}/iteration`.


**spec-optimization** (0 confirmed / 0 raw) — docs: docs/specs/optimization-standard.md

docs/specs/optimization-standard.md is highly accurate against the code; I found zero discrepancies. Verified: (1) the four traits LayoutOption/ConfigOption/BuildMode/HasOptimizationAxes and their exact signatures match kermit-iters/src/optimization.rs:26-64 and are re-exported from lib.rs:51; (2) the bench-axis prefix convention (ds_layout_*, ds_config_*, ds_build_mode, and reserved algo_* prefixes) matches both the trait doc-comments and docs/specs/bench-report-schema.md:142-149; (3) the CLI flag --ds-layout-hasher with Sip/Fxhash ValueEnum (main.rs:184-190, 208), the run_ds_bench_hash<H>/run_benchmark_hash<H> dispatch (main.rs:766,1067), the optimization_axes() merge (main.rs:869,1229), and the validation error message ("--ds-layout-hasher is only valid with --indexstructure hash-trie (or all); got --indexstructure {indexstructure:?}", main.rs:242-245) all match — and IndexStructureSelector::TreeTrie Debug-renders as "TreeTrie" exactly as the doc's example error shows; (4) the not-yet-implemented items the doc flags as hypothetical (--ds-config/--ds-build flags, define_multiway_join_test_suite_with_config!/_for_build_mode! macros) genuinely do not exist, so the doc is consistent with reality; (5) HashTrie's HasOptimizationAxes impl matches implementation.rs:205-216 (the doc's H::NAME.into() is a trivially equivalent simplification of the actual <H as LayoutOption>::NAME.to_string()); (6) the hash strategies sip/fxhash and rustc-hash="2" dep match hash_strategy.rs and kermit-iters/Cargo.toml:13; (7) schema_version is 2 (bench_report.rs:68), and the bench-report JSON example's "HashTrie/space" function id matches format!("{ds_name}/space") at main.rs:727,846; (8) the define_multiway_join_test_suite!(Type, Algo) signature and HashTrieSip/HashTrieFx aliases match macros.rs:268 and join_tests.rs:12-18; (9) all 8 entries in the "Where to look" table and all inline links resolve to existing files/anchors/sections. Paper-external claims (SIGMOD 2020 §3.3 seven optimizations, Table 6) are not auditable against the repo and were not flagged.


**spec-space** (5 confirmed / 5 raw) — docs: docs/specs/space-benchmarks.md

The doc accurately describes the two code areas it was pointed at: kermit-ds/src/heap_size.rs (heap-only byte accounting using Vec::capacity) and kermit/src/measurement.rs (SpaceMeasurement with Value=usize=bytes, BytesFormatter, and the iter_custom calibration/iters-saturation trap), and the Criterion default-features opt-out it implies is correct (kermit/Cargo.toml:20). However, the doc as a whole is badly stale: it is a 2026-03-16 'Criterion Output Specification' written against a planned standalone benchmark binary (kermit-ds/benches/space_benchmarks.rs with Exponential/Factorial input generators) that was never built and was superseded by the bench-consolidation CLI path. The entire 'Benchmark ID Mapping' table, the directory-layout examples, and every Space/Exponential|Factorial/{k}/{n} function ID are fictional — the implemented space benchmark emits a single `{IndexStructure:?}/space` function (e.g. TreeTrie/space) via `kermit bench ds/run --metrics space`. The group set is also wrong (the doc lists only TreeTrie/ColumnTrie and omits HashTrie, which the enum and run_ds_bench_hash both support), the '28 directories' count is unfounded, and the stated sample_size default (10) contradicts the CLI default of 100. The numeric/JSON shape claims (estimates collapsing to zero variance, sample.json times-are-bytes, tukey/change semantics) are plausible Criterion behaviour and not flagged. The sibling spec bench-report-schema.md correctly documents function IDs like `space/P`, making space-benchmarks.md internally inconsistent with the live spec set.


**usage** (5 confirmed / 5 raw) — docs: USAGE.md

USAGE.md is largely accurate on the per-command CLI surface: every documented subcommand (join, bench join/ds/run/list/fetch/clean, gen watdiv/lubm) and its flags, defaults, short forms, and the `bench gen` tunables all match kermit/src/main.rs; the CSV-header behaviour, the `--metrics` shape, the `--report-json` default path, the kermit-lab CLI subcommands and public API names, the `uv sync --group test` group, and all referenced links (docs/specs/bench-report-schema.md, benchmarks/README.md, BENCHMARKING.md, kermit/src/bench_report.rs schema_version=2) are correct. The doc's significant problem is that it consistently describes the system as having only two index structures (tree-trie, column-trie) and one algorithm (leapfrog-triejoin), when the code has three structures (ColumnTrie, HashTrie, TreeTrie) and two algorithms (HashTriejoin, LeapfrogTriejoin). This produces an explicitly false claim that 'LFTJ is the only concrete algorithm', an undercount in the `-i all` sweep description, an internal inconsistency with the hash-trie-only `ds_layout_hasher` axis it documents in the analysis section, and a `bench run --all -i all -a all` example that actually panics on incompatible (sorted-trie, hash-triejoin) pairs rather than running a clean Cartesian sweep.


**watdiv-preprocess-readme** (0 confirmed / 0 raw) — docs: scripts/watdiv-preprocess/README.md

scripts/watdiv-preprocess/README.md is accurate against the code. I verified every factual claim: (1) Outputs section — dict.json/dict.parquet (dict_builder.py:66-83 write_dict_files), per-predicate <predicate>.parquet with two Int64 columns named s/o (partitioner.py:51-68, pa.int64() + pa.table({"s":..,"o":..})), <parent-dir-name>-<sparql-stem>.yml (yaml_emitter.py:38-40, f"{parent}-{stem}" with '.'→'-'), and optional expected.json (pipeline.py:95-103). (2) Two console scripts watdiv-preprocess + sparql-preprocess (pyproject.toml:15-17 [project.scripts]). (3) WatDiv driver flags --input/--output/--base-url/--nt-name with default watdiv.10M.nt, globbing watdiv-stress-* subdirs, harvesting .desc into expected.json automatically (drivers/watdiv.py:17-54, expected.py:76-78 write_watdiv_expected). (4) Generic driver flags --input/--nt/--output/--base-url/--sparql-glob (repeatable, default '**/*.sparql')/--expected-json which copies verbatim and does NOT harvest .desc (drivers/generic.py:20,72-96; DEFAULT_SPARQL_GLOB='**/*.sparql'). (5) Core library run_pipeline in watdiv_preprocess.pipeline with both drivers as thin wrappers (pipeline.py:34, drivers delegate). (6) Scope claims — BGP-only SELECT, FILTER/OPTIONAL/UNION/subqueries fail loudly (sparql_translator.py:40-48 TranslationError); literals dictionary-encoded and partitioned (dict_builder.py:42-63, partitioner.py:36-68); unseen URI constants get a fresh dict ID rather than failing, becoming a c<id> singleton-trie no-match, with dict re-emitted when it grows (sparql_translator.py:88-112, pipeline.py:86-93). The const-atom rewriter and SingletonTrieIter referenced in Scope exist in kermit-algos (kermit-algos/src/const_rewrite.rs, rewrite_atoms; kermit-algos/src/singleton.rs SingletonTrieIter). The only loose phrasing is line 28 ('watdiv.10M.nt at --input') which reads naturally as 'at the --input directory' and is disambiguated by the explicit Flags entry (line 41, 'dir containing watdiv.10M.nt') and matches the code's own docstring (drivers/watdiv.py:21); not a contradiction. No discrepancies rise to a reportable finding.
